Katalog Berita (Halaman 43/57)

#2943. Open Thread: Spurs offering grants to local organizations through Operation Renovation

The NBA is known for its ties to community service. In each city where a team resides, numerous non-profits, businesses, and youth organizations receive support via the local franchise. When it comes to supporting local entities, the San Antonio Spurs go above and beyond. From the contributions of individual players (talking about you Harrison Barnes) to the Spurs organization as a whole, there is a balance of year round outreach and support.

"Spurs Give and Frost Bank (Frost) are inviting local organizations to apply for the 2026–27 Operation Renovation grant program, which supports facility improvements that create safe and inspiring spaces for youth to learn and play. Online applications for the 2026-27 Operation Renovation Grant are now open through Friday, Dec. 18 at 11:59 p.m."

Julie McCarty, Associate Vice President of Community Investments for Spurs Sports and Entertainment stated,

"Everyone deserves spaces where they feel welcome, supported and connected to their community. As we enter our ninth year of Operation Renovation with Frost, we're proud to support local organizations that do so much for San Antonio families and help improve the spaces they rely on every day."

Spurs Give and Frost have awarded over $1.3 million since 2018-2019. Every year, applicants are eligible to receive up to $100,000 for facility renovations.

Applications can be accessed HERE. Deadline to apply is Friday, December 18th. 2026.

Welcome to the Thread. Join in the conversation, start your own discussion, and share your thoughts. This is the Spurs community, your Spurs community. Thanks for being here.

Our community guidelines apply which should remind everyone to be cool, avoid personal attacks, not to troll and to watch the language.

#2936. Bucks vs. Knicks Player Grades: Ament and Jakucionis flash on both ends, Kuzma does not

The Milwaukee Bucks flashed some nice stretches, but simply weren't on the same level as the New York Knicks, going down 117-85. Without Tyler Herro, the Bucks struggled to get open looks against a slew of elite Knicks defenders, which let the Knicks play freely and get whatever they wanted. There's really not much more to it; at times, the teams looked like they were playing a different sport. Shrug. We move. Read our full summary of the game here and catch a six-minute audio recap on the Bucks+ podcast, Bucks In Six Minutes, below.

24 minutes, 14 points, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 0 turnovers, 5/9 FG, 4/6 3P, -10

Rollins played one of his best games of the season, frankly. Shot the ball with confidence when he was open, and I loved his ability to probe off pick-and-rolls and get deeper into the paint. There has been some criticism of Jenkins, saying he should have played Rollins more, and I agree; Rollins should have gotten around 30 minutes, given how well he was playing. Now, garbage time starting early may have impacted that, but still.

25 minutes, 16 points, 5 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 4 turnovers, 6/13 FG, 2/5 3P, -2

Jaquez was solid. He got off the chain in the third, scoring 12 of his 16 points in the period, including an instinctual backcourt steal after he sensed Karl-Anthony Towns was going to casually drop it off to Brunson without looking. Too many turnovers, though, which has been a theme lately.

25 minutes, 5 points, 5 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 block, 1/4 FG, 1/3 3P, -14

Just a dud game from Kuzma, who made very little impact on either end and, at times, looked like he wanted to be anywhere else. Namely, there was a play where the shot clock was winding down, and he had multiple chances to at least chuck something at the rim, only for the shot clock to expire while the ball was in his hands. And to be clear, the defender wasn't smothering him and making it impossible to shoot; he just… chose not to. Very odd.

27 minutes, 3 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 block, 2 turnovers, 1/7 FG, 1/6 3P, -24

Unfortunately, it was the same old story with Turner. He nailed the game's first bucket on a pick-and-pop three, but could not get his jumper to fall from that point forward. When I watch Turner, I see him doing a lot right in terms of positioning, but he seems to have lost a considerable amount of mobility, so guys can jump over and around him. That said, not having Herro there as a reliable pick-and-roll partner (and someone who demands attention) did Turner no favours.

17 minutes, 8 points, 4 rebounds, 2 blocks, 2 turnovers, 2/6 FG, 0/0 3P, +1

An encouraging performance from Ware. He looked more active and "into the game." I thought it was notable that he didn't take a three; it seemed like there was a directive to roll instead of pop, since his jumper hasn't been going in recently (or at all, really). Needs to build on that now.

23 minutes, 11 points, 4 assists, 1 steal, 1 turnover, 4/12 FG, 2/6 3P, -16

Still not shooting the ball terrificly, but there was a lot to like from Jakucionis' game. He made consecutive great reads in the third quarter getting downhill, drawing multiple defenders and, without getting too deep and risking a turnover, throwing cross-court dimes to Ament and Jaquez for threes. Had a few nice finishes in that same quarter as well. I also thought KJ's defensive intensity, especially guarding Brunson, was solid.

26 minutes, 3 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1/6 FG, 1/4 3P, -23

An off game for Burries, which are going to happen. He really struggled to navigate New York's long, tough defenders. This grade may seem harsh, but my expectations for Burries have risen considerably.

Respectfully, I don't remember one thing Green did.

22 minutes, 10 points, 2 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals, 0 turnovers, 4/8 FG, 2/5 3P, -13

The rook just keeps building. Ament is clearly trusting his three-ball on offence—and he's already started using the Pat Connaughton "catch-high, keep-high" shooting form—but the headline from this game was his defence. I mean, they put him on Brunson and, I can't believe I'm saying this, but he did a really good job! Basically, if Ament could prevent Brunson from getting his shoulders past him—which did happen a few times—he was able to get great contests on the Knicks superstar's shots. That said, Ament did mess up on two switches in the first half; his off-ball awareness remains a work in progress.

24 minutes, 8 points, 3 rebounds, 3/8 FG, 2/5 3P, -22

Dieng is starting to trust his jumper, which is good to see. Frankly, he could be a bit more aggressive at the rim. Try to use his size more effectively.

I do think Jenkins needs to work on his rotations a little bit, and he said as much postgame. I've seen some folks go a bit over the top about this, but yes, there's merit to the argument that seemingly as soon as guys find a rhythm, they can often end up on the bench. And in general, few units play consistently together for long stretches. For example, the Bucks' starters played a total of 10:47 together last night and posted a net rating of +18.0 in those minutes, per Hoops Junkie. At the same time, the point of this season isn't necessarily to max out on veterans playing together; it is to discover which pieces are worth keeping for the future.

Garbage Time: Bogoljub Markovic, Gary Trent Jr., Jericho Sims

Inactive: Kam Jones, Cormac Ryan, Pete Nance, Tyler Herro

The Bucks badly, and I mean badly, need Tyler Herro to look like a functioning team.

The Bucks lost the second and fourth quarters 64-26 combined. They also got destroyed on the boards, 59-32, and lost the possession game, 89-79.

Karl-Anthony Towns didn't shoot well from the field (5/14), but he attempted an absurd 14 free throws. Myles Turner's inability to provide any resistance was a major reason for that.

Jenkins does seem to have quite a short leash with Ware. He had a stint last night where he came in, made a defensive mistake on Towns, and got taken right back out, with Jenkins deep in conversation with him after he was pulled. I don't really know what to think of that, quite honestly. You could argue it's unfair that Turner seemingly gets way more leash despite playing "equally as bad." That said, I do think Turner does a lot of intangible things that don't show up on the stat sheet and that, despite his poor form, he is needed for 25-30 minutes per game for the Bucks to look like a functional basketball team. I do not necessarily feel that way about Ware.

Not sure if it's even worth mentioning, but did anyone else catch Ament stay on the ground a little longer after his and-one in the first quarter? It looked like he might have tweaked his ankle a bit. Clearly, it wasn't too bad since he played the rest of the game, but I guess there could be some lingering soreness. I'll be monitoring the injury report over the next week.

The Bucks get right back to it tonight at home against the Los Angeles Lakers. Catch the game at 7:00 p.m. CST on MY24.

#2929. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for the long haul

Global stock markets have been volatile as investors assess high bond yields and continued tensions between the U.S. and Iran. There are concerns about the impact of uncertainty in the Middle East on oil prices and consequently on inflation.

Investors who prefer to look beyond short-term noise and buy stocks with attractive long-term growth potential can follow the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts. The ratings of these experts are backed by in-depth research of a company's financials and thorough analysis of the factors affecting its business performance.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Semiconductor giant Nvidia (NVDA) reported stellar fiscal second-quarter results and reassured investors about continued strength in AI-driven demand for its chips. Moreover, Nvidia's FY28 revenue growth outlook of 70% was well above the Street's expectations.

Reacting to the Q2 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on NVDA and increased his price target to $300 from $288. "This is our Top Pick in the semis group, with a compelling product cycle, exceptional growth and valuation below peers," said the 5-star analyst.

Nvidia delivered impressive quarterly results despite supply constraints, Moore said, but he believes that the FY28 revenue and gross margin commentary mattered more, with a favorable outlook for both measures.

Moore said Nvidia's 70% revenue growth guidance for FY28 was above his own estimate of 52% and the consensus of about 40%. The top-line growth estimate was a "remarkable figure" given supply issues and Moore said he expects Nvidia to continue to address bottlenecks to deliver rapid growth.

Among other takeaways, the analyst said performance metrics for Vera Rubin chips were strong, with Nvidia emphasizing a 30x higher throughput per megawatt and 35x lower token cost compared with Grace Blackwell Ultra.

Moore ranks No. 159 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 24.60%. See Nvidia Statistics on TipRanks.

Ride-hailing and delivery platform Uber Technologies (UBER) was recommended by BMO Capital analyst Brian Pitz, who discussed the company's opportunity in the autonomous vehicle (AV) market in a recent research note that reiterated a buy rating and price target of $119.

The 5-star analyst highlighted Uber's transition into a diversified marketplace, with investors now focusing on its AV growth prospects. The key question is how much value can Uber generate as AVs become commercialized, Pitz asked.

"We continue to believe that Uber's AV strategy is evolving and will become a significant driver of revenue and profitability," the analyst said.

Importantly, Pitz believes that Uber can establish itself as a preferred mobility platform for AV makers, supported by its growing AV infrastructure capabilities and an expanding partner base. This would enable Uber to win a significant share of value as the AV market is commercialized.

Additionally, Pitz noted Uber's enhanced position in the AV value chain, with the company shifting from serving as a distribution channel for robotaxis to becoming a broader platform for AV mobility. The analyst discussed Uber's efforts to capture growth opportunities in the AV market, including higher capital spending, expansion into new markets and building partnerships beyond Alphabet's Waymo.

Pitz ranks No. 574 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of about 12.20%. See Uber Technologies Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Semiconductor company Marvell Technology (MRVL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the second quarter of FY27. But the company's increased FY28 guidance fell short of investors' high expectations.

In reaction to the Q2 FY27 results, KeyBanc analyst John Vinh reaffirmed a buy rating on Marvell Technology, with a price target of $400. The analyst noted that Marvell's results and outlook were driven by strength in its data center business. The Q2 FY27 data center business grew 46% year-over-year, to $2.17 billion, far above KeyBanc's estimate of $2.09 billion.

Management "highlighted accelerating AI networking demand across scale-out, scale-across and scale-up architectures, supported by strong 800G optical DSP demand, rapid 1.6T ramp, broader 51.2T switch deployments and higher TIA/driver demand," Vinh said.

Consequently, Marvell raised its data center revenue guidance, now expecting 60% growth in FY27 and more than 60% in FY28.

Regarding the recently announced $120 billion warrant arrangement with Google, Vinh said management argued that programs tied to the warrant are already priced into the custom outlook through FY28. Meanwhile, new programs and those in execution are expected to drive significant upside to the previous FY29 custom XPU revenue estimate of over $10 billion, Vinh said, citing management.

Vinh ranks No. 123 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 28.50%. See Marvell Technology Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2927. Lakers’ Collin Sexton trying to remain patient with finding rhythm

ATLANTA — As Collin Sexton tries to find his groove with the Lakers, coach JJ Redick has reiterated a sentiment during conversations with the veteran guard.

"He can want it so bad," Redick said of Sexton.

Collin Sexton brings intensity to the Lakers. AP Photo/Mike Stewart

That competitive fire, and sense of urgency, is what drew the Lakers and the 27-year-old guard to each other during the offseason when Sexton signed a two-year, $19 million contract with the franchise.

But Sexton is learning that it's going to take time for him to get where he wants with the Lakers.

Especially when factoring in the time he missed during the preseason and training camp as he recovered from a strained right calf.

The Lakers are starting to get a better, healthier version of Collin Sexton. NBAE via Getty Images

So after having one of his best games of the season in the Lakers' win over the Hawks on Monday, the former Alabama guard was once again reminded of the importance of patience.

"That's one word I've been using each and every day," Sexton said. "I've been praying to God every day to give me patience and allow me to continue to work my way into a role that I like but also work my rhythm and get back to where I feel like you know what I'm back. So I would say each and every day is going to have its ups, have its downs but I'm blessed to be able to go out there and healthy enough to go out there."

Sexton's been up more than down lately as he gets his rhythm back.

He had 18 points and 6 assists in the win over the Hawks, which marked the first time he scored in double figures and finished with a positive plus/minus in back-to-back games this season after having 17 points in Sunday's win over the Pistons.

"Maybe another week and a half, two weeks and I'll be back [to normal]," Sexton said. "Right now, these last two games felt really good. So I'm moving in the right direction."

Los Angeles Lakers head coach JJ Redick has emphasized patience with Collin Sexton. Getty Images

It's notable that Sexton's best games of the season have come when with either Luka Dončić or Austin Reaves sidelined.

He scored a season-high 21 points in the Oct. 28 win over the Clippers, which Dončić was sidelined for.

"He gets a little sped up, or defensively for us right now he's kind of just everywhere," Redick said. "And not like in a good way. So that's just some stuff going back to being able to practice and show film and just clean some stuff up at the end of the week. But clearly, we won two road games [now three after Monday's victory] with him having a big role. We we wouldn't have won those games if he wasn't part of that. So he's been great for us.

#2924. Patriots sign rookie tight end Tanner Arkin to practice squad

After 48 hours away, Tanner Arkin has made his way back to the New England Patriots.

The organization officially signed the rookie tight end to the practice squad on Wednesday. He cleared waivers the afternoon prior.

Arkin, 23, had been let go from the 53-man roster on the heels of last weekend's 45-27 loss to the Green Bay Packers at Gillette Stadium. The 6-foot-4, 262-pound blocking tight end now takes the open spot on the 17-man practice squad after the recent release of outside linebacker Jose Ramirez.

Arriving in Foxborough as part of the 2026 undrafted class, Arkin outlasted the NFL's cutdown deadline heading out of training camp. The former transfer from Colorado State to Illinois went on to appear in five games, including back-to-back starts, before finding himself on the league's transaction wire.

He played 60 snaps on offense and 41 snaps on special teams while also seeing time at fullback in the absence of incumbent Reggie Gilliam, who has since been designated to return from injured reserve.

The Patriots move further into the fall with captain Hunter Henry, No. 95 overall pick Eli Raridon and August waiver claim Cameron Latu on the active tight end depth chart. On the practice squad, Arkin joins September signing Tanner Conner at the position.

Mike Vrabel's 5-4 team will face Dan Campbell's 6-2 Detroit Lions in Germany on Sunday as part of the NFL international series. Kickoff at Munich's Allianz Arena is set for 9:30 a.m. ET.

#2922. NBA power rankings: Luka Dončić, Lakers on a tear as top team shifts again

It has been three weeks since the 2026-27 NBA regular season opened and there have already been three different teams to top this list.

That has been the state of play thus far, as no team has begun to separate itself as a dominant force – at least not yet.

But if there is one theme that has emerged, it's that potent offenses are ruling the day, and no player has been leading his team's scoring more than Los Angeles Lakers star Luka Dončić, who is putting up Wilt Chamberlain-like numbers.

Here are the Week 3 USA TODAY Sports NBA power rankings:

Though he has been inefficient, rookie Darius Acuff Jr. has been a bright spot. There haven't been many others.

As they have most of the season, the Bulls remain dead last in net rating and it's not even close, with an abysmally poor mark of -16.2. In fact, they're ranked dead last in several major metrics.

Practically all of their impact veterans are injured, and coach Will Hardy even appeared to mouth to himself on the sidelines during a recent game about how bad Utah has been.

Losers of seven consecutive, the Bucks have faced tough competition recently. Either way, the offense, which ranks 29th in points per game with 106.3, isn't capable enough to compete with below-average teams, either.

They simply have not found a third (or fourth) scoring option behind Michael Porter Jr. and Julius Randle.

As long as the Clippers continue to commit turnovers at the pace they do – 18.5 per game – the offense won't be able to sustain consistency.

It looks like that four-game win streak in the middle of the second week of the season was a mirage; the Pels have now lost three consecutive and allowed 138.5 points per game over their last two.

Since Tyrese Haliburton went down with a left hamstring strain, the Pacers have gone 1-6.

No team has a worse shooting percentage in the NBA than Charlotte, which is converting baskets at just 40%, a full 10 points lower than the Lakers (50.7%), the squad leading the category.

It looks like this team just won't be competitive without Paolo Banchero, who has now missed seven games.

There had been some promise – AJ Dybantsa has flashed some nifty moves at times – but a four-game losing skid has zapped any juice from these new-look Wizards.

It has been a wildly inconsistent start of the season for the Suns, who are suffering from the dip in scoring by Dillon Brooks. He's averaging 14.9 points per game, down from 20.2 last season.

In a recent game, the Hornets were missing three starters, two of whom are their best scorers: Kon Knueppel, Brandon Miller and Moussa Diabate. The full-strength Cavs lost by 12.

The Grizzlies have cooled off a touch since their blazing start to the season, losing three of their last four.

Things have certainly been better since that horrid start, and they have had some tough defensive luck, but defensive rebounding, consistency with their 3-point shooting and finding a secondary scorer to complement Giannis Antetokounmpo are still issues.

They've lacked pop and don't have a single victory over a team with a winning record.

After essentially alternating wins and losses, it appears the Raptors may be getting some good news, as Kawhi Leonard (knee swelling) is due to be reevaluated soon.

It's clear the Mavericks are going to have flashes of brilliance when Cooper Flagg and Kyrie Irving are in sync the way they've been this week.

They're continuing to share the ball, leading the NBA in assist-to-turnover ratio (2.10) and assists per game (31.7), and they've won four of their last five.

They've won four in a row. The offense has been solid, though certainly not as crisp as it was during their magical postseason run.

After topping this list last week, Houston went just 1-2 and the common thread was a starting five that lacked the scoring pop it had in the first two weeks of the season.

After dropping their first three games of the season, the Warriors have ripped off eight consecutive and Stephen Curry, at 38 years old, has played his way into the (very, very early) MVP conversation.

Without Jalen Duren (hamstring), Cade Cunningham's assist numbers are down significantly, from 9.9 per game last season, to 7.6.

They had a brutal stretch of games against the Lakers, Spurs and Thunder. They went 1-2 and now have Oklahoma City, the team Portland toppled Tuesday, in a two-day back-to-back.

With all their injuries, there are just going to be nights like Tuesday, when Shai Gilgeous-Alexander simply didn't have enough to outgun the shot-happy Trail Blazers.

Even with Anthony Edwards (knee) out for an extended period, the Timberwolves have remained competitive and Ayo Dosunmu, who's averaging 23.8 points per game over his last five, has stepped up nicely.

Perhaps Jaylen Brown allowed emotion to dictate his approach Tuesday against the Celtics. One other key issue in that loss: the Sixers uncharacteristically allowed turnovers to snowball into 21 points off of those giveaways.

In the first true test about whether Boston made the right choice to ship Jaylen Brown to the 76ers, Paul George erupted for 36 points and made six 3s while Brown struggled through early foul trouble to only 13 points.

They own the NBA's longest winning streak at nine games, Victor Wembanyama is back to posting absolutely monster stat lines and San Antonio is playing with excellent cohesion.

They may not be generating a ton of assists relative to their made field goals, but Luka Dončić (39.3 points per game) is on a historic start and overseeing the NBA's best offense, which is averaging 121.5 points per 100 possessions.

#2918. Giants-Commanders matchup: Giants’ offense needs to be better than it was vs. Philly

The New York Giants have a quick turnaround and little time to get ready to face the Washington Commanders on Thursday Night Football.

The Giants are currently 5-2-1 and leading the NFC East, while the Commanders come into the game with a 2-5-1 and in third place in the division. We should expect another tough game, and not just because the Giants can't seem to do anything easy.

The last time these two teams met, Jameis Winston was the Giants' quarterback and the game ended in a tie. The Giants' offense was in control of that game until an untimely fumble by Cam Skattebo. This time, the Giants have J.J. McCarthy as their starting quarterback, adding a much-needed element of athleticism and big-play ability to the Giants' offense.

The Giants are coming off of a tough (and sloppy) win over the Philadelphia Eagles, while the Commanders had a tough loss to the Los Angeles Rams. Both teams will be out to prove that the tie was a fluke, but can the Giants get their offense back on track after a rough showing against the Eagles' defense?

Giants' head coach John Harbaugh announced on Monday that McCarthy suffered a hip pointer in the Giants' win over the Eagles on Sunday. He also said that he expects McCarthy to play through the injury, though it's a painful one.

From the University of Pittsburgh Medical Center:

A hip pointer is a bruised hip. More specifically, it's a bruise near the top curve of your hip bone.

You get a hip pointer from falling onto your hip or having a direct hit to your hip. Though a hip pointer can be painful, with rest you can make a complete recovery.

A hip pointer usually does not need interventions like surgery. However, an athlete who gets a hip pointer needs to take a break while it heals. And, depending on how severe the hip pointer is, physical therapy might be beneficial.

Hip pointers are deep bruises, and the big question with regards to McCarthy isn't so much a risk of another injury or making it worse so much as how much the bruise will impact his play. It's possible that swelling from the injury could impact his gait or mobility, which could in turn impact his mechanics, accuracy, and ability to deliver the ball. Likewise, we don't know whether McCarthy will be as effective a runner (or scrambler) with the injury.

We should expect that the training staff is doing everything reasonable to manage the swelling and pain, but it's a factor the Giants will need to consider. We could see the Giants try to lean further into their running game with Cam Skattebo back (also confirmed by John Harbaugh), as well as the quick passing game to try and limit the stress on McCarthy's body.

The health of the Giants' starting quarterback is the most important piece of the puzzle, there's no argument about that. However, we simply can't ignore how poorly the Giants' offensive line played against the Eagles. The offensive line, and the interior offensive line in particular, was simply bad for long stretches where the Giants' blocking was neigh-nonexistent.

The Giants surrendered 6 sacks on the afternoon and both McCarthy and Winston often had to throw under pressure. The run blocking was, if anything, even worse.

While left guard Jon Runyan Jr. was the biggest offender, there wasn't any one player who was the sole reason for the unit's poor performance. In fact, it's more useful to view an offensive line as a unit than as five players to be assessed individually.

Likewise, there were a myriad of issues that cropped up on tape, but they were shared by all five linemen and not just one player's problems.

At times linemen would let their hands drop low, giving up inside leverage, allowing Eagles' defenders to control them. Other times, their punches would be late or wide with their hands landing outside defenders' framework and denying the blocker the opportunity to win inside leverage. There were other occasions where the Giants' blockers would drop their heads and lunge into contact, or conversely play too upright. In either case, they were either beaten quickly or put on skates and driven into the backfield.

We do also need to acknowledge that the Eagles boast one of the best defensive fronts in the NFL. It's possible that the Giants were pressing in the face of a talented opponent, but rather than raising their game they were allowing their technique to slip.

That's unacceptable coming out of a bye week.

That said, there might be a couple silver linings for Week 10. First and foremost, the Commanders' defensive front isn't nearly as formidable as the Eagles, and they've been struggling with injury. Daron Payne and Jevon Kinlaw are both on the injured reserve, and Johnny Newton will be playing in his second game back from pec surgery.

The Giants might be more likely to play within themselves against a front they've matched up against before.

Second, the short week might work in the Giants' favor as well. One of the most important parts of succeeding on Thursday Night is to do the basics well. There simply isn't time to focus on wrinkles or complex strategy and simply executing the foundation of your scheme can be the difference. That focus on execution and technique is what the Giants' line needs most.

It's possible we could see the Giants' offensive line rebound in Week 10 simply because there isn't time to overthink things. And in that, the good news is that while there were far too many bad blocks and breakdowns, there were also some very good blocks against the Eagles. The Giants' line can play at a high level, they just need to consistently execute their fundamentals.

The Giants had a tough time against the Commanders' defensive front in the first match-up between these two teams. However, that front will look a bit different this time around, with defensive tackles Kinlaw and Payne out, and Newton back in.

Newton only played 20 snaps against the Los Angeles Rams, but did get three tackles in that limited action and is coming off of a five-sack season. He can't be ignored, but the bigger threat is likely at the second level for the Commanders. Their Vikings-inspired defense frequently lines up in five-man fronts to gap out opponents' run games, allowing linebackers Frankie Luvu and Sonny Styles to flow to the ball, drop into coverage, or blitz.

It's effective when it works, however there are a couple potential areas that the Giants can exploit.

First and foremost, if (and that's a loadbearing if) the Giants' blockers will need play with more consistently good technique. If they're able to do so, there are opportunities to use alignment and misdirection to turn Styles' and Luvu's speed against them. Cam Skattebo will never be able to outrun either, but their speed can be used to pull them out of position, forcing bad angles and the kinds of tackle attempts that Skattebo routinely pinballs off of.

Greg Roman loves using man-gap blocking in his run schemes, but also using pulling blockers to change the math on the play-side. He also uses secondary blockers like FB Patrick Ricard, TE Isaiah Likely, TE Chris Manhertz, and even WR Malachi Fields to add to the box count on the Giants' side of the equation. That all is well known, which also creates the opportunity to use pullers as misdirection pieces, using a move blocker to pull a linebacker out of position and get them to flow away from the play.

The Los Angeles Rams did something a bit different that could be instructive for the Giants. Rather than spreading the Commanders out, they used condensed sets and tight alignments to match up against Washington's five-man fronts and create additional gaps. That forced Washington to concentrate their defenders tightly, and the Rams were able to use the traffic to slow the linebackers and allow their own blockers to work to the second level.

Where the Giants' backs only had 74 total yards (21 carries) on the ground against the Commanders in Week 5, the Rams had 103 on 26 total carries. The Rams have a much better run blocking offensive line than the Giants do, but there are still lessons to be learned there.

Likewise, the Giants can also use play-action and mesh points to help slow the Commanders' defense.

The Giants have one of the lowest play-action rates in the NFL (roughly 20 percent, or 8th lowest), as well as one of the lowest rates of early-down passing (10th lowest). That should make early and neutral down play-action passes particularly effective, as the Giants' tendencies are to hand the ball off when the quarterback and running back mesh. In other words, the Commanders' second level players will be forced to come up and respect their gaps, creating a void in the middle of the field that can be exploited by the Giants' receiving weapons.

Dan Quinn's defensive philosophy is well-understood by this point in his coaching career. His scheme has been based on hybrid fronts under coverage shells that are fluid but based in Cover-3 mechanics. He wants his defensive backs playing downhill and able to have eyes in the backfield so they can swarm to the ball or attack it in the air.

There was something of a shift in 2026 with the hiring of Daronte Jones as defensive coordinator. Jone had spent the last several seasons with Brian Flores in Miami and was tapped to bring that fire-breathing defensive scheme to Washington. The Commanders don't blitz nearly as often as the Vikings (nobody does), but they are blitzing relatively often for a Dan Quinn defense. They also tend to do so out of Cover-4 shells, much like Flores, though they also use those looks to disguise their intentions for post-snap rotations to Quinn's favored Cover-3 shells.

The two looks have distinct advantages and disadvantages that are well known. The idea is that the pressure from the pass rush will force offenses to check down rather than attack the coverages' weaknesses. However, if the blocking can hold up, there are yards to be found.

The Los Angeles Rams used Puka Nacua to consistently exploit the voids in the Commanders' coverages, and the Giants should be going to school on how Sean McVay used his route concepts to quickly work Nacua into the soft spots in Washington's coverage. Malik Nabers is a similar caliber route runner as Nacua and he had a big day against Washington back in Week 5. The first time around, Nabers hauled in 11 catches (on 20 targets) for 121 yards and a touchdown. Malachi Fields is also turning into a "zone buster" as he continues to improve his route running and rapport with J.J. McCarthy as the weeks go bye. The duo of Fields and Nabers — particularly working Nabers into the slot — should prove potent against Washington's Cover-4 looks.

Isaiah Likely also had a big day against the Commanders back in Week 5, catching 11 of 13 for 94 yards and two touchdowns. Athletic tight ends are the bane of Cover-3 defenses, and the Giants could have a better feel for Washington's tendencies by now. There could be big plays to be had for Likely, as well as Jalin Hyatt or Darnell Mooney, streaking down the seams between the outside corners and the deep safety — at least as long as the Giants accurately anticipate the rotations to (or out of) Cover-3.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2912. How LJ Cryer plans to step in for injured Brandon Williams off Warriors' bench

How LJ Cryer plans to step in for injured Brandon Williams off Warriors' bench originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

HOUSTON – After sweeping a six-game homestand to extend their win streak to eight straight, the Warriors on Wednesday will begin a strenuous five-game road trip without one of their most important bench pieces.

Backup point guard Brandon Williams scored four points on three shot attempts in only one minute and 39 seconds during the first quarter of the Warriors' 51-point blowout against the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday night. But he exited the game as quickly as his speedy first step because of feeling something wrong in his left hamstring. Luckily for him and the Warriors, the worst-case scenario was avoided.

An MRI on Tuesday revealed Williams has a mild left hamstring strain and will be re-evaluated in one week.

Williams is averaging 10.4 points in 16.1 minutes per game. He scored 14 points in his team debut and has reached double figures in five of the nine full games he has played. Without Williams, the Warriors will turn to another undersized point guard, LJ Cryer, who is in his second season on a two-way contract.

"A little bit of maybe less layups, more threes," Cryer said Wednesday during Warriors shootaround at the Toyota Center ahead of their game against the Houston Rockets. "But at the end of the day, he puts the ball through the basket driving, and I put it through on the perimeter. So it's a different dynamic, but as long as the ball is going in, it's all good."

Williams can put the ball on a string, blow by you and jelly a highlight reel layup in a flash. He's shooting 50 percent at the rim this season (7 of 14) and 47.9 percent overall within 10 feet of the hoop (23 of 48). Listed kindly at 6-foot-1, his game is in the paint and not beyond the arc, where he's shooting 30.4 percent (7 of 23), which is an improvement from his 23.2 3-point percentage last season with the Dallas Mavericks.

The opposite can be said for Cryer, who is generously listed at 6-foot.

Cryer has appeared in eight of the Warriors' 11 games for a total of 50 minutes this season. The 25-year-old has taken 25 shots and 16 of them have been 3-pointers. He has taken just one shot at the rim, two within 10 feet and four inside of 16 feet from the basket.

Despite their differences, Cryer says he has learned a lot watching Williams this season. He isn't trying to be him, but he is trying to impact the second unit like Williams has now that he will be given a bigger opportunity.

"He has a really good burst, and I feel like I have a good burst too," Cryer said. "I may not be as fast as him, but it's just about using my speed as a more of an advantage to get to the paint a little more than I usually do."

In 20 minutes Monday night, Cryer scored 10 points off the bench in place of Williams, including two threes and two floaters to showcase his all-around game as a scoring spark plug.

Starting the road trip in Houston will be special for Cryer, too. He starred at Morton Ranch High School 27 miles from the Rockets' home court and finished his college career at the University of Houston his final two years, where he was a First Team All-Big-12 selection and Wooden All-American as a fifth-year senior.

Cryer's immediate family and a handful of close friends will be in attendance for Wednesday night's game, and he plans to be the same player he always has been in front of them.

#2911. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2909. Patriots up-to-date depth chart: Who is on the roster right now?

The New England Patriots' regular season is underway, which also means that their roster and practice squad will undergo near-constant change. For reference, between the opener and regular season finale alone last season, the eventual conference champions made 102 different transactions.

It remains to be seen how many there will be in 2026, but things can get confusing quick. So, in order not to get lost amid any potential movement, here is our handy up-to-date Patriots roster and depth chart.

#2906. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2904. Puka Nacua won’t go on IR and Davante Adams could be back soon

The Los Angeles Rams will not put Puka Nacua on injury reserve with his knee injury according to head coach Sean McVay, but that does not mean that the receiver will miss fewer than four games.

ESPN's Sarah Barshop reports that the decision to keep Nacua on the 53-man roster is due to "semantics" and that Nacua missing IR is likely with regard to the Rams only having so many spots and too many players being injured.

There is also the chance that the Rams are thinking Nacua can suit up in Week 14 against the San Francisco 49ers, in which case he would only miss three games. This could be wishful thinking however.

Week 14 may be the earliest Nacua rejoins the Rams as they reportedly expect him to miss "extended time". Whatever the reason(s), Nacua may have avoided a mandatory missing of four games by staying off IR, and that could be a positive development for the Rams. On the bright side, McVay indicated that Davante Adams may be able to return after the bye week, which would bring him back in Week 12 against the Packers.

On the season, Nacua has in some ways been the Rams offense with over 700 receiving yards and four touchdowns. The veteran wide receiver has also hauled in 70 catches on 94 targets. Davante Adams is the only other receiving weapon "close" to Nacua with 30 receptions, 480 yards and matches Nacua with four touchdowns of his own.

The only other receiver on the Rams with 30 or more receptions is Kyren Williams and he's a running back.

There are real questions for what L.A.'s offense will look like without Nacua, and at the time of this reporting/tweeting from Barshop, the Rams may only have to live in that realm for a few weeks, with Nacua expected to be back sooner than initially projected.

Of course, if Terrance Ferguson can have an injury setback, so can Nacua. Nacua isn't out of the woods yet in terms of how much time he'll miss, but reports are more optimistic on Wednesday then they were Sunday/Monday after Nacua's injury was first reported.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2903. Milwaukee Bucks vs. Los Angeles Lakers Preview & Game Thread: No rest for the wicked

After a brutal thrashing by the New York Knicks, the Milwaukee Bucks are back at home tonight to face the Los Angeles Lakers in the second game of a back-to-back. For the Bucks, there is little to build on from last night in New York beyond the games of Nate Ament and Kasparas Jakucionis, who should both see more of the floor after strong performances. The Lakers, meanwhile, are 10-2 and first overall in the Western Conference. This will be the toughest test of the year, and it couldn't come at a worse time for the Bucks, who are still looking for their second win of the season.

There is no gentle way to say this: the Bucks will be outmatched at home unless someone can make a stand in the frontcourt. The Lakers' offense is relentless and currently the best in the NBA, scoring 122.4 points per 100 possessions. Much of that comes from their two-headed monster in the backcourt: Luka Doncic and Austin Reaves. Reaves, who inked a big extension this off-season, is averaging 25.3/3.4/4.6, and with those numbers, could only be second fiddle to Doncic, who is off to an absolutely torrid pace of 39.3/7.8/7.9 on nearly 68% true shooting. For years, this is what it felt like to run into Giannis, and now the Bucks find themselves on the other end, hoping to make it a game—a task that proved too big in New York.

As if the dynamic duo in the backcourt wasn't enough, the Bucks will have their hands full in the paint with Walker Kessler. Kessler is making an early-season push for an All-Defense team as opponents are currently shooting just 47.1% against him at the rim and 41.1% overall. Couple that with 2.5 blocks per game and a double-double average, and you have an elite starting centre (something that feels so distant for Milwaukee). Given how bad the night was in New York, it feels like the only way the Bucks will stand a chance in the frontcourt is with an injection of youth and energy. Kel'el Ware looked more competent on defense and had a couple of close shots roll out, while Nate Ament looks more comfortable by the minute. Despite all this, the Lakers are not exactly running away with games, with five of their wins by single digits. This can be blamed in large part on the defensively challenged Doncic and Reaves; if the Bucks want a chance, they will need to attack that duo early and often.

Milwaukee's injury report hasn't been submitted yet because the team is on a back-to-back, but Tyler Herro will be the one to watch.

Matisse Thybulle has been asked to guard many players on the perimeter all season long, and so far has been incredibly effective. The Bucks will need to be firing on all cylinders if they want a chance to beat the Lakers, but if Thybulle can be a disruptor, the Bucks could be in for another long night.

Who: Milwaukee Bucks (1-10) vs. Los Angeles Lakers (10-2).

Milwaukee needs a shot in the arm, so let's look to Big Nate Ament to deliver against the Lakers and bring some life to the team. Ament will get his first double-double of the season and reinvigorate the Bucks as they go for the upset win at home!

#2896. Jaylen Brown fell flat in first post-trade duel with Celtics: ‘I’m a bit frustrated’

In the weeks leading up to the first Celtics-Sixers matchup of the season, Jaylen Brown had been hyping up the reunion. But just six minutes into Tuesday night's game, his long-awaited rivalry debut began to unravel.

Brown admitted it himself after Philadelphia's 123-101 loss to Boston, acknowledging he and his Sixers teammates didn't do enough to match up to the Celtics during the marquee matchup.

"I'm a bit frustrated, honestly," Brown told reporters, per CLNS Media. "I wanted to perform better for my team. Foul trouble kind of derailed me a little bit, but it happens. It's unfortunate. But now we gotta just move on to the next."

Brown established himself as the aggressor from the jump. He nailed Jordan Walsh's throat before flushing a two-handed dunk, bulldozed Derrick White and was called for an offensive foul, then intercepted a pass intended for Jayson Tatum. He also missed his first two shot attempts and picked up two fouls before checking out with 5:33 remaining in the first quarter.

In his first attempt to attack Tatum, Brown pump-faked a three, dribbled into a spin move and lost control of the ball, sending it out of bounds. Paul George and Neemias Queta followed Tatum's lead, pointing toward the other end of the floor in reaction to Brown's turnover.

PHILADELPHIA, PA – NOVEMBER 10: Jaylen Brown #7 of the Philadelphia 76ers and Jayson Tatum #0 of the Boston Celtics look on during the game on November 10, 2026 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Brown was forced to Philadelphia's bench, where he watched the Sixers struggle to keep pace.

Boston's bread-and-butter — 3-point shooting — came in handy throughout the night, and it began early. In the first quarter, the Celtics nailed four of their first 11 threes while the Sixers only converted one of their first seven attempts. The disparity carried over into the ensuing quarters, as both teams continued firing.

Brown, usually an above-average, reliable 3-point shooter, went just 1-for-4 himself. He played just over 25 minutes, well below his 35:48 average and his lowest total of the season so far. Brown's four personal fouls, three of which were accumulated by halftime, forced Sixers head coach Nick Nurse to play him less than four minutes in the third quarter.

Crucial minutes in which Brown could've used to turn the tide were wasted as he battled foul trouble.

He turned in an underwhelming performance by his standards, as Brown finished with a season-low 13 points along with five rebounds, two assists, two steals and a block. On Sunday, he told reporters in Philadelphia he had nothing else to tell Tatum or the Celtics except "check the ball up." And on Tuesday, Brown was overshadowed by Tatum's second-career poster dunk over LeBron James and George's 36-point outburst.

Needless to say, things didn't go as planned, which left Brown with no choice but to tip his cap to his adversaries.

"Credit to Jayson. He played well tonight," Brown said. "Credit to the Celtics. They executed. They shot the hell out of the ball, and they were the better team tonight."

Brown helped stir the pot moments before tipoff. As both teams greeted each other at center court, Tatum approached Brown and gave him a friendly, one-handed pat on the chest. Brown, meanwhile, spun the game ball on his finger without even acknowledging Tatum's presence — a moment reminiscent of Kevin Garnett and Ray Allen's icy exchange in 2012.

It seemed clear Brown had no interest in dapping up Tatum.

"To be honest, I wasn't paying attention," Brown responded.

Once the game ended, Brown continued to keep his distance from Tatum. He shook hands with nearly every Celtics player before heading back to Philadelphia's locker room, once again leaving his former co-star out of the postgame exchanges.

For those keeping track at home, that's twice Brown ghosted Tatum on the court (three times if you include their preseason warm-up session at TD Garden on Oct. 10).

And for the Celtics, their trip to the City of Brotherly Love was a success.

They checked all the boxes needed. All the boxes they could've hoped to check.

Tatum and George combined for 57 points. Boston responded to Philadelphia's game-tying 20-7 run with a 12-2 spurt that carried into the fourth quarter. And Payton Pritchard also caught fire, giving the Celtics 18 points off the bench, while knocking down four consecutive 3-pointers in the second half to fuel the team's runaway victory.

For Brown, even 10 seasons spent with the Celtics, including years alongside many of the team's current players, couldn't provide much of an advantage.

PHILADELPHIA, PA – NOVEMBER 10: Jaylen Brown #7 of the Philadelphia 76ers dunks the ball during the game against the Boston Celtics on November 10, 2026 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Brown dismissed the idea that familiarity with Boston's personnel made any difference.

"Does it make it easier? No. It's basketball at the end of the day," Brown said. "It's five-on-five. You gotta come out and execute, and they were better than us tonight. They executed better than us tonight, and they outplayed us. So that's on us. We gotta improve. They're one of the top teams in the league, and they've been together for a while, so we got a lot to work on over here. So we gotta get to work."

The Celtics leaped over the Sixers as the No. 1 seed in the Eastern Conference on Tuesday night.

The high-intensity, playoff-like duel between Brown and Tatum never materialized. Instead, Brown and the Sixers were forced to settle for a classic Boston beatdown, similar to the many Brown experienced plenty of times throughout his decade spent with Tatum by his side.

Philadelphia got outclassed. They allowed George to score more points than any opposing player has scored against them, dropped to 21st in the league in 3-point percentage by shooting 29.7 percent against Boston, and gave Sixers legend Allen Iverson, who sat in attendance, little to celebrate.

When George drained his game-leading sixth 3-pointer of the night to put the Celtics ahead 111-87 and force a Sixers timeout, Joel Embiid hung his head and grabbed his hair in disbelief.

At that moment, everyone in the building knew that was all she wrote.

Brown only needed three words for his blunt, honest assessment.

"Tonight was humbling," Brown said. "We got outplayed, outexecuted. So we go back to the drawing board and get ready for the next one."

For the Celtics, Sixers, Brown and Tatum, the next one won't be until their Jan. 21 rematch at TD Garden.

#2891. Broncos Star Named NFL’s Top Projected Free Agent

The Denver Broncos got this good on defense by developing players into stars — both through the draft and by nabbing underrated free agents.

One draft pick under head coach Sean Payton who has developed into a star is outside cornerback Riley Moss, a 3rd-round pick in Payton's first draft in 2023.

Moss, to put it lightly, is about to get super-duper paid this offseason — by the Broncos or someone else — and Bleacher Report's Kristopher Knox put him at the top of his 2027 NFL Free Agent Big Board following Tuesday's NFL trade deadline.

Moss came in at No. 11 on Bleacher Report's Top 50 and was the second-highest-rated player at his position behind Dallas Cowboys cornerback Joey Porter at No. 9 overall, where he's predicted to receive a 4-year, $110 million contract.

While Moss might not sniff those lofty contract numbers, he's going to get pretty darn close. It would be an incredible come-up for a player in his third year as a starter and in the final season of a 4-year, $5.4 million rookie contract.

The Broncos are currently 7-2 and on a five-game winning streak — they're also in line to make the playoffs with Moss as the starter opposite NFL All-Pro cornerback Patrick Surtain for the third straight season.

"A 2023 third-round pick out of Iowa, Riley Moss has established himself as a quality perimeter starter in Denver's defense," Knox wrote on Wednesday. "The 26-year-old has been heavily tested while playing opposite Patrick Surtain II and has rarely flinched. He's a rangy corner at 6'0″, 193 pounds, who will only turn 27 in March."

Porter's top two possible suitors were listed as the Detroit Lions and Washington Commanders — teams that might also be counted on to go after Moss if he becomes available.

The DNVR Denver Broncos Podcast went over what a contract extension might look like for Moss before the season, projecting a 3-year, $55 million extension.

If Moss hits the open market after a good-to-great season, that final number could leap up another $5 million to $10 million.

From DNVR: "For Riley, the projection is 3 years, $55 million … that's probably about the ceiling right now. He's certainly not getting $20 million per year. Even $18 million is a little too close to that, so we're erring on the high side. If these guys hit the open market next year, they're getting this kind of money. You do get a little bit of a discount when you sign them early, so there's no reason it wouldn't happen here."

Moss was one of the NFL's top earners in the league's annual performance-based incentives program following the 2025 season — the bonus system that pays players with low salaries and high snap counts.

Moss scored a $1.136 million bonus after starting all 17 games in 2025. It ranked Moss No. 12 among all NFL players who received bonuses, with Chicago Bears cornerback Nahson Wright coming in 1st with a $1.441 million bonus.

In 2024, Moss started 14 games after missing 3 games due to injury, and in each of his 2 seasons as a full-time starter, the Broncos have made the playoffs.

Moss' contract paid him approximately $1.2 million in 2025 — meaning his bonus almost doubled his annual salary.

"Some exceptional players on that list — including Riley Moss toward the top," Broncos fan Ron Caphill wrote on X. "Hopefully, Broncos Country appreciates and understands this."

This article was originally published on HEAVY

The post Broncos Star Named NFL's Top Projected Free Agent appeared first on HEAVY.

#2890. Knicks Bulletin: ‘You know he doesn’t jump real high, but he’s long’

As everybody knew before tip-off, the Knicks ended up beating the Bucks on Tuesday.

Wednesday, however? That's an entirely different beast, as New York hosts Cleveland.

A different beast, I mean, as they wear red threads instead of green ones. Other than that, this one's also ending in yet another easy Knicks' dub.

On Karl-Anthony Towns' rebounding instincts, positioning and physical advantages:

"He's just got a nose or a knack for the ball. You know he doesn't jump real high, but he's long, and he understands angles.

"KAT's in that category when it comes to rebounding. We emphasize it quite a bit, and we tell guys, 'You gotta put bodies on bodies.' So if our small is on other people around him or putting bodies on bodies, it's a one-on-one contest for KAT, and he's gonna win most times."

On Towns recording a double-double in each of the Knicks' first 10 games:

"He continues to be a double-double machine at a high level."

On the Knicks' defensive physicality against Milwaukee:

"Our group, they were pretty efficient tonight. I thought our physicality on the defensive end of the floor was at a pretty high level, and they did it the right way. They did it without fouling. Which was a lot of fun to watch. The point-of-attack defense, with Landry [Shamet] and especially Deuce [McBride] on the ball, was unbelievable. Deuce was our defensive player of the game. Could have gone to Landry as well. But just a fantastic job at the point of the screen in pick-and-rolls. [The Bucks] had two 13-point quarters and I'm sure they missed some shots, but I thought we also impacted their shots with our play."

On Josh Hart's ankle recovery and whether he could have played against Milwaukee:

"From what they've told me, correct me if I'm wrong, if he was not sick, he would (have played)."

"We'll go with who we go with until we feel that it's time to go to the next guy. So again, it's not anything negative right now on Jose or even Tyler when he's out. They just got to be ready to play when their number's called, and both of them are trying to do that."

On Tyler Kolek earning the edge over Alvarado in the backup point guard competition:

"Right now, Tyler's played pretty good basketball. You know, we've strung some games, and, you know, again, those guys are close.

"The reality is Jose got hurt. Tyler gave us some good minutes, but at the end of the day, I sat them both down and I told them: 'Jose, you're our backup because you ended the season as the backup, but both you guys helped us win last year. Jose in the Finals. Tyler in the Cup Finals. So I feel like both you guys can play and contribute at a high level. So both you guys got to be ready because a change can happen at any time.'"

On sticking with Kolek unless his performances warrant another change:

"And so I'm gonna go with Tyler, and if I think I need to make a change because Tyler's not getting it done in my eyes, then I'll go back to Jose."

On Alvarado and Kolek supporting their teammates despite fluctuating playing time:

"So they just have to both stay ready and make sure that they continue to do what they're doing. They both, when they're not playing, they both have done a heck of a job cheering for their teammates and trying to uplift everybody."

On the Knicks' offensive rebounding on Tuesday:

"Obviously our players, they're trying to compete most times when it comes to going after a shot attempt. So I give our players a lot of credit."

On why the Knicks don't need extraordinary individual performances from their starters:

"With the way our team is built we don't need anybody to do anything extraordinary. Just go, like Landry said, be yourself, play hard. It's not necessarily always how you start games. It's how you finish."

On allowing the flow of games to determine his rotation:

"The game will dictate it. I go in with a minutes sheet. I don't know if we've followed the minutes sheet once so far this year or let alone even last year. It's just a cheat sheet of different lineups that you think you can run at the right time based off who's out there to defend, who's out there handling the ball, who's out there setting screens and all that other stuff. We let the flow of the game dictate what's gonna happen."

On the Knicks returning to their fundamentals and improving defensively:

"I just think that we're doing a great job of getting back to the basics. We started off slow, and now we're picking it up. While the shooting isn't doing great — I could be the first one to tell you about that tonight — but I think we're doing a great job of executing right now at a high level, and our defense is there. At the end of the day, the offense will catch up. The defense is something you got to establish. We're doing a great job of that right now."

On approaching Willis Reed's franchise record for consecutive double-doubles to start a season:

"I didn't know that. I just know that this team right now, we're on a good winning streak, we're playing good basketball, and that's the most important thing. That's what the fans really want to hear about."

On maintaining the same approach whether starting or coming off the bench:

"I try to keep things as consistent as possible. In the past in my career, if it was a spot start or a stretch of them, I think I made the mistake of trying to change too much. Or think I had to change a lot. Or do something differently. Or be something different. Just because I'm starting the reality is it's the same basketball game. You can create different reasons as to why it's different. But for me, and I think the whole goal for anybody, is setting yourself up for success and for me that just looks like keeping things as standard and concise as possible."

On the Knicks' constantly changing roles and the responsibility to contribute wherever needed:

"Like we've always said, with this team, that's the kind of special benchmark of our group is it could, that could change from night to night. from person to person. There might be something required of me differently, tonight versus tomorrow night, might be making shots, might be guarding this guy. It might be being a great teammate on the bench and picking up somebody who's struggling, or, whatever the case may be, all these roles are constantly evolving and rotating. And it's all of our jobs collectively to just fill the voids that need filling when needed."

On embracing the Knicks' team-first culture regardless of how many minutes he plays:

"That's just our culture here. No matter if it's one minute, 30 seconds, 30 minutes during the game. That's what we focus on. That's what Mike has preached to us since he's got here and that's just a big thing all of us hold our hat on."

#2889. 49ers injury updates: Nate Hobbs returns to practice

The San Francisco 49ers injury report is out for Wednesday. The following players won't practice:

WR Mike Evans (Achilles)CB Renardo Green (back)CB Deommodore Lenoir (foot)

WR Deebo Samuel (hamstring)CB Siran Neal (knee/foot)CB Upton Stout (shoulder/neck)DT CJ West (limited)

The team officially opened the practice window for West to return from the injured reserve, which is why he's on the injury report.

Cornerback Nate Hobbs is a full go. With both starting cornerbacks out, and the only other option being rookie Ephesians Prysock, the Niners may give the veteran an opportunity. Lenoir and Green not practicing also explains why the 49ers added Jack Jones to the practice squad.

Last week, defensive coordinator Raheem Morris said Hobbs has the versatility to play inside or outside. Shanahan said, "Nate's a real good NFL corner. Seen him at a number of places. We've scrimmaged against him. I've played against him in games. He didn't have a long camp, but he had long enough with us that you could see him effective inside, effective outside. His versatility is great. Where he's needed."

#2888. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.
