#2818. Canada’s ambassador warns Trump tariffs are driving up costs, threatening U.S. investment

Canada's ambassador to the U.S. warned Wednesday that President Donald Trump's tariffs are driving up investment costs and discouraging business expansion, as new Federal Reserve research points to more price hikes for American consumers next year.

"Uncertainty increases your cost of capital," Ambassador Mark Wiseman said at the B20 summit, a gathering of global business leaders in Washington hosted by the U.S. Chamber of Commerce.

"If you're going to build a new plant, add a new ship, spend money on R&D, the more certainty you have, the more likely you're going to do that," said Wiseman, a former BlackRock executive who became Canada's ambassador in February.

His warning comes as Washington and Ottawa struggle to resolve a tariff quarrel affecting one of the U.S.'s largest trading relationships. The two countries exchanged roughly $570 billion in goods during the first nine months of 2026, according to U.S. Census Bureau data. That's an increase from the same period in 2025, the first year of Trump's second term, but lower than the comparable nine months during the previous three years, when Joe Biden was president.

U.S. customs duties calculated on imports from Canada reached $10 billion in 2025, compared with $400 million in 2024, according to the Congressional Research Service.

An October study by New York Fed researchers found that nearly 90% of tariff costs were passed on to U.S. importers, with about a quarter of tariff increases eventually reaching consumer goods prices and further price pressures expected in 2027.

The findings come as Labor Department data showed consumer prices rose a higher-than-expected 0.4% in October, adding to inflation concerns after Republicans' sweeping midterm losses.

Trump has defended his tariffs as necessary to protect U.S. manufacturers and workers, accusing Canada of discriminatory trade practices that disadvantage U.S. exporters.

The two governments have resumed negotiations after talks collapsed in August, with discussions focused on reducing tariffs on Canadian steel and automobiles. Trump has imposed duties of up to 50% on certain Canadian products, prompting retaliatory measures targeting billions of dollars in U.S. exports.

Wiseman called for the removal of tariffs imposed under Sections 232 and 338 of U.S. trade law, saying a resolution would help businesses make long-term investment decisions.

The political stakes have shifted following the midterms, with Democrats preparing to challenge Trump's tariffs when they take control of Congress in January.

Still, Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney said Tuesday that the Trump administration remains Canada's principal negotiating partner.

"We've had comprehensive free trade with the United States since 1988," Wiseman said, describing Canada's objective as returning "as close as we can to free trade" while creating the certainty businesses need.

European ambassadors echoed those concerns at the B20 summit. Germany's ambassador to the U.S., Jens Hanefeld, said businesses could adapt to tariffs but struggled with an "ever-changing regulatory environment" that forced companies to spend more on lawyers to navigate shifting trade rules.

Speaking on the same panel, France's ambassador, Aurélien Lechevallier, urged greater cooperation on AI and energy rather than continued trade disputes.

"We've spent, I think, a lot of time the last years working and dealing with regulations, tariffs," he said. "Businesses are eager to do more business."

#2817. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2816. Observations after Sixers lose 1st Brown vs. Tatum matchup, George scores 36

Observations after Sixers lose 1st Brown vs. Tatum matchup, George scores 36 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Round 1 of the new-look Sixers vs. the new-look Celtics decisively went Boston's way on Tuesday night.

In the first meeting between the teams since the July trade that sent Jaylen Brown to Philadelphia and Paul George to Boston, the Celtics earned a 123-101 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

The Sixers dropped to 9-3 and the Celtics improved to 9-2.

George was the game's brightest star, scoring 36 points on 14-for-22 shooting. Jayson Tatum added 21 points.

Tyrese Maxey's 24 points led the Sixers. Brown was hampered by foul trouble in his heavily anticipated first outing against Tatum. He posted 13 points, five rebounds and two assists in 26 minutes.

The Sixers will host the Raptors on Thursday night. Here are observations on their blowout loss to the Celtics:

To no one's surprise, Brown and Tatum guarded each other to open the night.

VJ Edgecombe defended a former teammate in George, who scored Boston's first four points with a pair of pure jumpers. On the other end, Maxey made the Sixers' first three field goals.

Judging by volume, the home fans cared most about the much-scrutinized Brown vs. Tatum matchup. The crowd was excited early when Brown snagged a steal and then dished to Maxey for a fast-break three-pointer. The fans also dialed up the noise when Brown sized up Tatum in isolation. He wound up dribbling the ball off his foot and out of bounds. Tatum pointed to signal it was Celtics ball.

Boston's star forward had a productive first quarter, posting 11 points. He scored an easy two when he used a back screen to break free from Brown and convert a layup that put the Celtics up 11-7. Boston began 9 for 12 from the floor as a team.

As for Brown, he sat shortly after being called for his second foul with 6:48 left in the first quarter.

Brown re-entered at the start of the second quarter. The Sixers still struggled to eat into Boston's lead.

A Sam Hauser triple extended the Celtics' advantage to 12 points. There was some audible frustration in the building after Brown missed two free throws on the next possesion.

The Sixers' deficit hit 16 points on a Hugo Gonzalez jumper. Meanwhile, the Sixers missed 12 of their first 14 three-point tries. While some of those shots were open looks that simply didn't go down, the Sixers also rushed up several contested jumpers before they made Boston's defense do serious work.

In the bigger picture, it wouldn't be remotely surprising if shooting stands out as a weak area for the Sixers come the trade deadline. They entered the game ranked 27th in the NBA in three-point frequency outside of garbage time and 22nd in three-point accuracy, according to Cleaning the Glass.

Joel Embiid (16 points, six rebounds) had an injury scare late in the second quarter when he fell hard after missing a layup in transition. He rose gingerly and limped through the final 30 seconds or so of the first half.

The Sixers' star big man was fine to start the third quarter and even drained a long-overdue three on the opening possession. He'd been in a terrible outside shooting slump (3 for 32 from deep on the season) before that shot.

Brown's foul trouble persisted. He picked up his fourth personal with 10:40 to go in the third quarter.

The Sixers stormed back without him and their defense played a large role. After LeBron James rumbled in for a layup, Dean Wade stonewalled Payton Pritchard on the perimeter to force a shot-clock violation. Wade later canned a three to even the contest at 76-all.

The Celtics responded and Tatum led the charge. His third-quarter exploits included a highlight slam on James and a top-of-the-key three to build Boston's lead back up to nine points.

Pritchard buried a three on the first possession of the fourth quarter and the Celtics' advantage kept growing. George celebrated a triple that stretched his team's lead to 100-82. The Sixers' spirits were considerably lower as they headed to their bench for a timeout. As a Sixer, George only had one higher-scoring game (39 points) than his performance Tuesday.

George and Tatum were each excellent in Philadelphia. Outside of Maxey, who finished 8 for 12 from the field, the Sixers didn't receive similar games from their stars.

Brown will be back in Boston on Jan. 21 when the Sixers visit the Celtics. He'll try to even the score.

#2815. Purdue Football: Depth Chart Review – Defense – Maryland

I've never seen a defensive coordinator lose their job based on a single quarter, but that's what happened to Kevin Kane on Saturday. Now, there's a long list of defensive transgressions that had the Boilermaker defensive coordinator living on a razor's edge coming into the game, but things looked promising in the first half. The defense was getting the ball back to the offense, and the offense was squandering golden opportunities to put the Terps away. They were looking for a reason to quit, but the Boilermakers' red-zone offense kept giving them hope.

Maryland's third quarter sealed Kane's fate. They scored 3 touchdowns on 13 total plays, including a 75-yard touchdown run and a 44-yard touchdown pass. One of the 3 touchdowns came from a Ryan Browne fumble that put the ball on Purdue's 2-yard line, so it's hard to blame them for that one, but the other 2 were pretty much inexcusable. The third quarter bled into the 4th quarter, with Chris Durr Jr. making Purdue's defense look like a bad J.V. unit on his 87-yard touchdown reception that drove a stake directly into the heart of the Kevin Kane regime.

It's one thing to lack talent, and don't get it twisted, Purdue lacks talent on defense, but it's another thing altogether for a defense to quit on the field. The defense quit on that play, and it was all over for Kane. It will be interesting to see what the defense looks like under the yet-to-be-announced interim defensive coordinator, but something tells me he'll struggle in similar ways.

These guys aren't getting any bigger or faster; hopefully they don't quit again.

CJ Madden: Snaps – 31 – Tackles – 4 – Sacks – 1 – T.F.L. – 1

Keyshawn Burgos: Snaps – 17 – Tackles – 1

Curt Neal: Snaps – 34 – Tackles – 6 – T.F.L. – .5

The stats look weird in this one because Maryland only needed 45 plays to score 34 points. It continues to be musical chairs at strong-side defensive end, with CJ Madden getting the nod here with 31 snaps despite Keyshawn Burgos getting the start. Madden recorded the Boilermakers' only sack, and Burgos was credited with a quarterback hurry.

Jeremy Lewis started on the weak side but didn't record a single pressure and made only one tackle. In theory, he's Purdue's best pass rusher. That theory was flawed on Saturday.

Mayland threw 18 passes, and Purdue managed to pressure Malik Washington only 3 times. That's not good enough.

On the interior Curt Neal and Ian Jeffries received the majority of the work. 101 of Maryland's 144 yards came from runs in the A gap, which isn't ideal, but much of that blame falls on the linebackers and safeties. Credit where a sliver of credit is due; only 3 of Purdue's 14 missed tackles were issued to the defensive line.

This group lacked talent on opening day and continues to lack it 9 games in.

Charles Correa: Snaps – 48 – Tackles – 5 – T.F.L. – .5

Chales Correa played his worst game at Purdue. He made 5 tackles and missed 3 tackles in the run game. He hasn't lived up to expectations this season. I don't see anything on film that suggests he's a Big Ten-caliber starting linebacker. He's not fast enough to be a sideline-to-sideline linebacker, and he's not strong enough to be a run-stuffing box linebacker. He misses too many tackles and doesn't make up for it with big plays.

In terms of the run defense, JoJo Hayden may be the only Boilermaker who can walk off the field with his head held high on Saturday. He led the team with 11 tackles and missed only 1. He hasn't been the problem. He's been the best middle linebacker on the team by a wide margin, but he needs to be paired with someone faster than Correa. Hayden should always play inside and only in zone coverage because he's not fast enough to cover running backs and tight ends man-to-man. According to PFF, he ended up matched up against a wide receiver twice in this game, resulting in 2 receptions for 55 yards a touchdown.

Tre Moore is playing about as well as you'd expect Tre Moore to play. He's a replacement-level player in terms of talent, but he plays hard.

I don't know, y'all, linebacker is pretty bleak, to be honest. I'd keep Hayden and rethink the rest of the position group this offseason. This defense needs a massive injection of speed.

Don Saunders: Snaps – 45 – Tackles – 3 – Targeted – 2 – Rec Allowed – 2 – Yds – 11

Hudari Hines: Snaps – 44 – Tackles – 3 – Targeted – 5 – Rec Allowed – 5 – Yds – 82

Smiley Bradford: Snaps – 18 – Tackles – 1 – Targeted – 1 – Rec Allowed – 0

Mister Clark: Snaps – 10 – Targeted – 1 – Rec Allowed – 1 – Yds – 87 – Td – 1

I've defended Hudari Hines this season. He's been adequate as a 2nd cornerback … for the most part. That wasn't the case on Saturday. Maryland completed every throw they directed his way, with 4 of the 5 going for first downs. There's no need to go after Don Saunders when you can attack Hines instead.

Speaking of Saunders, he's been a bright spot in an otherwise dismal defense. He had another solid day in coverage but missed 2 tackles in run defense. He hasn't been the problem this season.

I thought Mister Clark's return would help the defense. Turns out I was wrong. He's the defensive back on the hook for Maryland's 87-yard touchdown. Bradford was better in coverage, but he also missed a tackle as a run defender.

At the end of the day, when Maryland needed a reception, they got a reception.

John Slaughter: Snaps – 48 – Tackles – 4 – Targeted – 2 – Rec Allowed – 1 – Yds – 6

Justin Denson Jr.: Snaps – 38 – Tackles – 5 – Targeted – 1 – Rec Allowed – 1 – Yds – 4

This group was OK. They only missed 1 tackle, but when the other team was breaking off huge plays at will in the 3rd quarter, both were M.I.A. Slaughter's been decent, but there's a reason why both of these guys have bounced around to multiple teams in their careers. Their former teams have always found a player they prefer more. That makes sense.

#2814. Scott Colom wins Mississippi Senate election over incumbent Republican Hyde-Smith

Democratic nominee Scott Colom won the Mississippi Senate election over Republican incumbent Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith, the Associated Press reported Wednesday.

Colom, a local district attorney, will be the first Black senator from Mississippi since the post-Civil War Reconstruction Era.

His projected victory — which was not considered likely by national analysts before Election Day — is the latest in a series of stunning wins by Democrats this midterm cycle.

Two sources close to Colom's campaign said Hyde-Smith called him late Wednesday afternoon to concede.

"While this is not the outcome we worked for, or hoped for, I respect the decision of Mississippi voters," Hyde-Smith said in a video posted on X.

"I want to congratulate Scott Colom and wish him well as he takes on the tremendous responsibility of representing our great state," Hyde-Smith said.

Democrats now will have a majority of at least 55 seats in the Senate, having flipped at least eight seats from Republicans.

Alaska is the only remaining Senate election to be called. In that state, Democratic nominee Mary Peltola, a former House member, has a narrow lead over Sen. Dan Sullivan, the Republican incumbent.

"Blue Mississippi is here," Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, D-N.Y., said in a post on X.

"Scott Colom is the only person who could've gotten this done. I knew it from the moment I met him," Schumer wrote.

In addition to the Senate, Democrats have won more than enough seats to take control of the House of Representatives when the next Congress begins in January.

#2812. Jensen Huang affair in D.C.: Resignation calls and moves to tighten Nvidia China exports

Members of Congress are calling on Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang to resign, and momentum is building to subpoena the executive, whose company is a leader in advanced artificial intelligence chips.

Senate banking committee member Kevin Cramer, R-N.D., doubled down Wednesday on his call for Huang to resign, and a key Democratic House oversight panel member and House Republican piled on.

"As a matter of his own conscience, he should resign," Cramer told CNBC, in response to revelations that Huang had an affair with an alleged Chinese spy as the chipmaker he co-founded was given permission from the U.S. government to sell its technology to China.

Huang, who leads a company that's been a chief beneficiary of the artificial intelligence boom, on Sunday admitted to having an affair with a woman whom news outlets have reported U.S. intelligence concluded was a Chinese spy. The revelation is sending shockwaves through Washington, with lawmakers now fearing that U.S. AI dominance could be at risk.

"What bothers me at the end of it is what we don't know is what she knows about him that can be utilized," Cramer said, referring to Alice Huang and Jensen Huang, who are not related. "This is a guy that's right now so vulnerable to extortion, blackmail. I just don't think, as a national security matter, we can run the risk."

Cramer's comments come on the deadline set by a trio of House lawmakers who requested Huang to testify before their committees on Nov. 18.

GOP Reps. Brian Mast, chair of the House Foreign Affairs Committee; John Moolenaar, chair of the Permanent Select Committee on China; and Rick Crawford, who leads the House intelligence panel, called for Huang to appear in a Nov. 4 letter. Congress members in the majority party also have subpoena power, should they choose to use it.

Mast and Moolenaar told reporters on Wednesday afternoon that they had not yet heard from Huang and that they were giving him until the end of the day to respond.

Asked whether the committees were considering subpoenaing Huang if he doesn't agree to appear, Mast said "yes."

Moolenaar, when asked the same question, said "all three committees have subpoena power, and I know all three committees have a very serious interest in getting to the bottom of this and having transparency around this."

"We are going to continue to pursue this," Moolenaar said.

The lawmakers are far from alone in their criticism, concern and outright shock over the affair. And the scrutiny is only expected to intensify with Democrats poised to take over both chambers of Congress in January. Huang has been close with President Donald Trump during the president's second term, traveling on Air Force One to China in May.

"Jensen Huang should resign from Nvidia," Rep. Suhas Subramanyam, D-Va., a member of the House Oversight Committee, told CNBC. Subramanyam is the top Democrat on the panel's Subcommittee on Military and Foreign Affairs.

"I didn't agree with his sentiment that there's no downside to exporting chips to China," he said. "But I never thought that he might be having an affair with a Chinese spy who was helping smuggle chips into China at an alarming rate."

Rep. Don Bacon, R-Neb., who is retiring from Congress after sometimes bucking Trump, also said the CEO should step down.

"From the little I know, yes." Bacon told CNBC in response to whether Huang should resign. "I mean, he allowed himself to get compromised by the Chinese government. Not good."

Huang was notably opposed to strict export controls on AI chips to China, which ruffled feathers with China hawks on Capitol Hill.

Democrats are widely expected to launch myriad investigations into the White House and industry when they assume control of both chambers of Congress in January. Nvidia is now likely to be at the center of those probes.

"There's two parts to this investigation that [need] to happen somewhere immediately," Subramanyam said. "One is what was going on with Nvidia and China, and two is why the administration did nothing about it even though they appeared to have known, and what the administration knew, and what influence the money from Nvidia and Jensen Huang had on this administration, essentially staying silent."

Rep. Jay Obernolte, R-Calif., co-chair of the House AI task force, said Tuesday he hopes that the Huang scandal can avoid becoming a distraction in talks about imposing export controls on advanced AI chips.

The situation may give Congress additional ammunition as it tries to pass a suite of bills in this year's National Defense Authorization Act to tighten export controls on advanced AI chips.

Congress has still not passed the annual defense bill, which is a top priority for lawmakers in the lame-duck session.

Those bills include the AI Overwatch Act, the MATCH Act and the Chip Security Act — all of which are aimed at preventing U.S. chip technology from falling into adversarial hands.

Sens. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., and Jim Banks, R-Ind., in a statement following the revelation of Huang's affair, urged their colleagues to include the AI Overwatch Act. Warren, the incoming Senate banking committee chair, sponsored the bill in the Senate with Banks.

"Huang's confirmation of a relationship with a suspected spy for China's intelligence service makes these national security concerns even more urgent and raises important questions NVIDIA must answer about the extent to which these activities were related," the senators said. "It is time for Congress to take decisive action to pass key bills like the AI Overwatch Act in the NDAA that will cut off pathways through which China accesses advanced chips."

And Subramanyam warned that what Nvidia does now — and whether Huang remains atop the company — could affect its relationship with Washington and whether lawmakers crack down.

"If Nvidia finds out that he knew about what was going on and does nothing about it, then Nvidia is making a choice and us as regulators and as watchdogs need to defend the American people," he said.

#2809. Nvidia prepares to oust Jensen Huang, WSJ reports — stock sinks

Shares of Nvidia fell more than 9% in after-hours trading on Wednesday after The Wall Street Journal reported that the company's board is preparing to oust CEO Jensen Huang.

The news landed minutes after The New York Times reported that Huang had an extramarital relationship with another executive at the company. Nvidia's board is preparing to meet as soon as Wednesday to determine Huang's future at the company, according to the Journal.

The alleged relationship was with Kimberly Powell, vice president of health care at Nvidia, according to the Times' report. Powell's bio on the Nvidia website says she's "responsible for the company's worldwide healthcare business, including hardware and software platforms for accelerated computing, AI, and visualization that power the ecosystem of medical imaging, life sciences, drug discovery, and healthcare analytics."

Huang and Powell were reportedly captured on camera being affectionate with one another at a hotel in San Jose, California in March, according to the Times.

Nvidia and Powell did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment. Heller, the crisis PR firm Huang reportedly hired as early as last year, did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment.

The report comes days after Huang admitted to an affair with a woman who is believed by U.S. intelligence of being a Chinese spy. The CEO was romantically involved with Alice Huang, according to The Financial Times, who U.S. intelligence officials believe is a spy for China's Ministry of State Security, The New York Times reported.

Nvidia's board of directors has opened an independent investigation and hired the law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to look into the spying allegations.

"I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did," Huang wrote in the message to Nvidia staff on Sunday. "I am deeply sorry."

Most of Huang's Nvidia shares are co-owned with his wife, Lori Huang, and are held in trusts. About 800 million shares are held by those trusts. Huang holds about 70 million shares on his own and has limited economic control over the company.

Rumors of an affair between Huang and Powell have circulated for years, in part because Powell lacks a background in health or science, according to a former employee who asked not to be named because of the sensitivity of the matter.

Healthcare is a smaller business at Nvidia. The company makes chips for medical devices and MRI machines, but they are a minor in comparison to the revenue generated from its advanced AI systems and graphics processing units, or GPUs. In recent appearances, including at the company's annual GTC conference, Powell has spoken about how Nvidia's AI technology can help accelerate drug discovery, assist providers and improve clinical efficiency. Powell has been leading the group since 2017.

Nvidia's stock is down 7% this week and is on pace for its sixth straight weekly slide, reflecting broad concern on the sustainability of the artificial intelligence buildout. The AI infrastructure trade was hit particularly hard last week after the midterm elections, as Democrats won sweeping majorities in both houses of Congress, and dozens of ballot measures past that could make building data centers more difficult.

Huang has become synonymous with the AI industry, and he is often referred to by his first name ,"Jensen," by customers and rivals. Nvidia has been one of the primary beneficiaries of the AI boom since its chips are used to train models and run large AI workloads.

—CNBC's Lora Kolodny and Kif Leswing contributed to this report

#2808. Eric Morris faces alma mater Texas Tech at Oklahoma State, where he never won as player or OC

STILLWATER, Okla. (AP) — Eric Morris never won a game as a player or offensive coordinator for Texas Tech at Boone Pickens Stadium, now home for the first-year Oklahoma State coach whose alma mater is coming to town for a significant Big 12 game late Saturday night.

"Really good memories when I played 'em at home," Morris said this week. "Really bad memories when I came to Boone Pickens Stadium."

What he hopes for now is some lasting memories for the resurgent Cowboys (6-3, 4-2 Big 12, No. 25 CFP). There should be a raucous atmosphere when they pretty much have to win for a realistic chance to get to the Big 12 championship game — after not winning a single league game the past two seasons.

"This is a core memory that they'll have for the rest of their life, so I'm excited for them," Morris said.

Morris, 4-0 at Boone Pickens Stadium as head coach including his opener over then-No. 6 Oregon, lost twice there as offensive coordinator for the Red Raiders and was also 0-2 as a player He caught a 12-yard touchdown pass from Graham Harrell in 2007, though Oklahoma State won 49-45 in a game that featured the likes of Michael Crabtree, Danny Amendola and Dez Bryant.

No. 10 Texas Tech (8-1, 5-1, No. 12 CFP) is coming off a 53-48 home win over West Virginia when Thomas Castellanos threw five touchdowns in a game that ended after midnight local time. After winning the highest-scoring game in the Big 12 this season, the Red Raiders now go to Stillwater to play in that same late-night ESPN broadcast window.

Oklahoma State, the Big 12 leader with 44 points and 534 total yards per game, is coming off a 66-28 win at Kansas State when Drew Mestemaker threw four TDs and ran for another score. That came a week after a 51-49 loss at Iowa State on a game-ending field goal.

While defending Big 12 champion Texas Tech is alone in second place in the league standings behind No. 2 Utah (9-0, 6-0, No. 2 CFP), Oklahoma State is one of four teams with two league losses.

"The objective anytime we touch the ball is we want to score the football," Morris said. "You do that in a lot of different ways and I don't think either one of these teams is one-dimensional right now, which I think causes some issues.

"But some of these games get weird and tricky," he said. "And it goes the opposite way and it's a low-scoring game, right? I've been in those before, too. It'll be fun."

During his senior season for the Red Raiders in 2008, Morris matched his career high with 10 catches for 97 yards and a touchdown against Oklahoma State in a game at Lubbock.

Mestemaker came with Morris from North Texas, which last season made the American Conference championship game before a loss to Tulane that kept them out of the 12-team College Football Playoff.

Now they are playing a meaningful November game in the Big 12.

"It's already a big-time game, it's his alma mater," Mestemaker said. "The emotions are there. If you can't get up and be hyped for a game like this then you're probably playing the wrong sport."

Morris' alma mater is the next game on the schedule. The Cowboys then finish the regular season at Arizona State and against Kansas.

"Obviously, there's a ton of history between me and this place," Morris said. "I think anytime you try to make anything about yourself or the past, you know, bad things happen to you. Two good ball clubs that are playing at a really high level right now. So, it should be an entertaining one."

Defensive back Mo Horn, one of two Texas Tech transfers on the Oklahoma State roster, said the Cowboys will play fast and confident with so much at stake.

"It's a big game for both sides," Horn said. "Win or lose, someone's probably leaving this game knowing that the season's almost over."

#2806. Sixers, Brown have a ‘humbling' night in loss to Celtics

Sixers, Brown have a 'humbling' night in loss to Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Whatever Jaylen Brown and the Sixers dreamed up for their first matchup against the Celtics soon flew out the window on Tuesday night.

Brown was whistled for two early fouls, the Sixers fell behind and their early-season flaws were on full display in a 123-101 loss at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

"We've just got to get better," Brown said. "Tonight was humbling. We got outplayed, out-executed. So we go back to the drawing board and get ready for the next one."

Over 26 minutes, Brown had 13 points on 4-for-9 shooting and five rebounds against the team that traded him away this summer.

As for the endless dissection of his relationship with former co-star Jayson Tatum, Brown didn't have much to add Tuesday. There was no public reconciliation, no obvious on-court animosity and generally nothing to satisfy fans hoping for some kind of fireworks on that front.

"I'm just focused on doing what's best for my team," Brown said. "Everything else … to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson; he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics. They executed, they shot the hell out of the ball and they were the better team tonight."

Tatum scored 21 points, two of which came on a third-quarter slam over LeBron James. Paul George continued his fantastic start as a Celtic by dropping 36 points and draining six three-pointers.

The Celtics dominated from beyond the arc, shooting 22 for 49 (44.9 percent). The Sixers went just 11 for 37 (29.7 percent) on another night of underwhelming offense. The team sits 22nd in the NBA in offensive rating, 24th in three-point makes per game and 21st in three-point percentage.

"I think that we've got some guys that are obviously shooting much lower percentages than their last season or their career average — almost a handful of guys," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "And I don't think it's because they're shooting fallaway, totally contested ones. I think they're shots we want them to take. So I think they'll continue to take 'em and I'm pretty sure those will even out into their career averages."

Joel Embiid is at 37 percent from the floor and 4 for 33 from three-point range (12.1 percent).

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center got through an injury scare when he rolled his ankle late in the second quarter and hit a three early in the third. It was his first long-range make since the Sixers' home opener back on Oct. 22.

"It's a work in progress," Embiid said. "I think the biggest thing is just not thinking. I'm probably overthinking it. The rhythm of the game is different. I'm so used to having to create for myself, so it's a little bit different.

"But I think I'm slowly getting better. You could see as soon as I made a three, the next shot in the short corner … I didn't even think about it. I just shot it and let it fly, and I made it. … I think it's just about taking what's in front of you. Make or miss, keep working on your game. It's going to get there at some point."

Even with the Sixers second in the Eastern Conference standings at 9-3, Embiid believes the team's defense has "slacked off a little bit" in recent games.

"I don't really look at the record," he said. "I just look at the process of, 'Are we getting better every day?' We can always do a better job at all phases of the game."

"They're one of the top teams in the league and they've been together for a while," Brown said. "We've got a lot to work on over here, so we've got to get to work."

The Sixers will face the Raptors on Thursday night in Philadelphia.

They'll have to wait until Jan. 21 in Boston to see the Celtics again.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said. "I was looking forward to playing well, but s— happens. Just get ready for the next one."

#2805. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2804. Matt LaFleur "shocked" Vikings acquired Kirk Cousins for only a third-round pick

Packers head coach Matt LaFleur was Kirk Cousins’ position coach when the quarterback came into the league.

Now the two are set to match up on opposite sidelines again after the Raiders traded Cousins to the Vikings on Tuesday.

LaFleur told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that he’s expecting Cousins to start Sunday’s matchup between Minnesota and Green Bay.

“Yeah, he’ll be out there. He’ll be starting. Fairly confident in that,” LaFleur said. “Obviously, he’s got familiarity with their system. Even if he didn’t, he’s a really intelligent guy and I think he’s been playing at a high level.”

What did make LaFleur raise an eyebrow was the trade compensation, as Minnesota sent a third-round pick to Las Vegas for Cousins.

“I was surprised they didn’t get more for him, quite honestly — I was shocked what they traded him for,” LaFleur said. “It was mind-blowing to me.”

While Cousins played under Vikings head coach Kevin O’Connell in 2023 and 2024, LaFleur said what Cousins has been doing with the Raiders this year still has relevance when putting together this week’s game plan.

“You definitely watch some of that, see what he’s had success with, what he’s comfortable with,” LaFleur said. “Obviously, he’s got a long track record with that offense with all those years in Minnesota with Kevin. And [there are still] a lot of the key pieces that were there with him.”

In nine games for the Raiders this year, Cousins completed 64.1 percent of his passes for 2,340 yards with 20 touchdowns and eight interceptions.

#2803. Princeton's Kam Mercer officially signs with UC Bearcats basketball

Almost six months after his commitment was shown on ESPN's SportsCenter, Princeton High School senior Kameron "Kam" Mercer returned to the same room to officially sign with the Cincinnati Bearcats on Nov. 11.

It was no surprise, as Mercer has been at UC games and was one of new coach Jerrod Calhoun's priorities when he arrived in town in late March.

Mercer helped the Vikings win the Ohio Division I state title on March 22, then Calhoun was introduced as UC's head coach the following day. Mercer had been offered by previous coach Wes Miller's staff in eighth grade. Not long after his introductory press conference, Calhoun was on the phone with Princeton coach Bryan Wyant, asking if his staff had a chance with Mercer.

The sales pitch succeeded and Mercer has been "all-in" on UC and Calhoun. After UC's difficult 63-61 loss to Minnesota, Mercer posted on X: "In him I trust" with a picture of himself with Calhoun.

During his signing ceremony, he became emotional while talking about his parents, Craig and Nicole Mercer. His mother came to the podium to comfort her 6-foot-5 son.

"I believe so much in Calhoun and what he's done," Mercer said. "I know he took a tough loss to Minnesota. I'm not really worried about one game. I'm super excited to get to the next level and play for him. I'm a guy that's going to come in on Day One and be a leader. Somebody that wants to win. I want to become a winner I want to become an NCAA champion."

Mercer led the Princeton Vikings to a state crown as a sophomore, averaging 14.2 points and 5.4 rebounds per game with 3.4 assists and 2.4 steals per contest. Playing at Huntington Expression Prep in West Virginia as a freshman against older talent, he averaged 11 points, 6 rebounds and 5 assists. He moved up a year scholastically to join the Bearcats for 2027-28.

He also attended future teammate Josh Tyson's commitment to UC in September. Tyson played against Mercer at Lakota West but is now playing at La Lumiere in Indiana.

Princeton coach Bryan Wyant has been at numerous UC basketball practices and has incorporated some of their strategies into his Vikings. Wyant believes doing so will give Mercer an advantage as a freshman Bearcat.

For now, Wyant is very bullish on Mercer.

"I think he has a chance to be one of the most dominant players that we've seen in the last 20 years," he said. "I've only been here 20 years, so that's all I can speak for. I'm talking about actual production on the floor. If he can keep progressing, I think he can have a chance at one of the special seasons that we've seen over the last 20 years."

#2802. Fernando Mendoza ready for challenge

Fernando Mendoza's latest lesson learned about life in the NFL: Things can change quickly.

Mendoza said he had "no inking" the Las Vegas Raiders were discussing trading starting quarterback Kirk Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings on Tuesday. Mendoza said he was at his locker on Tuesday when he heard his mentor was being shipped out.

Of course, that meant, Mendoza, the No. 1 overall pick in the draft, was being promoted to the starting quarterback position. Mendoza met with media members Wednesday after his first practice as the starting quarterback.

Mendoza called this a "once in a lifetime opportunity" and he is excited to play with his "brothers" starting Sunday at home against the Super Bowl champion Seattle Seahawks.

Mendoza made a point to heap praise on Cousins and his family for taking in as a essentially a family member. He called Cousins, a 15-season veteran, an ideal mentor and he said the veteran didn't hold back anything while trying to help him get used to the NFL.

The Raiders have lauded Mendoza for his preparations and he credited Cousins for teaching him that.

Raiders' coach Klint Kubiak said Mendoza was prepared in his first practice and he noted the team is not going to "peel back" anything against Seattle. thus, Mendoza will be asked to run the full playbook in his starting debut.

#2801. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2800. Dolphins make Josh Uche a team captain after three other captains go on injured reserve

Before the season, the Dolphins named six team captains. Three of those six are now on injured reserve, which left an opening that defensive end Josh Uche will fill.

Dolphins head coach Jeff Hafley announced today that Uche was chosen after consulting with the other team captains, who felt Uche was deserving of wearing the C on his jersey.

“They came to me and they wanted to name another captain, so I listened, and we named Josh Uche a captain today,” Hafley said. “A guy who has really done a tremendous job on and off the field. He is another guy who has been an outstanding leader for our young guys. He’s a guy who last year toward the end of the season was not even active, and now he’s a guy that people have to game plan around. He’s playing the run well, getting his pressures and his sacks, and he’s been an unbelievable teammate and great help to me.”

Dolphins quarterback Malik Willis, running back De’Von Achane and defensive lineman Zach Sieler are all captains who are now on injured reserve. In addition to Uche, the captains still on the active roster are left tackle Patrick Paul, center Aaron Brewer and linebacker Jordyn Brooks.

#2798. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2797. Zac Taylor: Duke Tobin is "really apologetic" after backing out of trade with Eagles

On Tuesday, the Bengals backed out of a trade for edge rusher Arnold Ebiketie after it was revealed that the deal might cost Cincinnati with a future compensatory draft pick.

During his Wednesday press conference, head coach Zac Taylor explained the situation from the perspective of Bengals director of player personnel — and de facto General Manager — Duke Tobin.

“Obviously, we tried something yesterday,” Taylor said. “Duke was very explicit with me when he came and spoke to me about how he wanted this portrayed. I’m not comfortable doing it, but I’m going to do what he asked. We did make an effort to put some depth in there in place of Cashius [Howell]. As we got into it, it was going to be more costly than we initially anticipated.

“He’s really apologetic about it and wants it made clear — Philly was a great trade partner. It was on our end. Certainly, we have a ton of confidence in our front office and how they do things. They’ve given us the players that have allowed us to be 6-2 right now, and playing at such a high level. So, we’ll put that behind us and move forward, and just get excited for this game on Sunday. Our locker room is in a great place right now.”

Taylor noted Tobin didn’t really tell him how everything went down and he didn’t ask the question.

“It doesn’t really matter to me. It probably took three minutes of my day yesterday away from watching tape,” Taylor said. “So, got a lot of faith in how those guys operate and we’ll just move forward with it.”

With that, the Bengals didn’t make any deadline moves to improve the team. Taylor said he and Tobin like where the team is.

“I think you can talk to the players in the locker room, they really like where we’re at,” Taylor said. “So, this is a team with a lot of confidence right now. I know it’s a fun period of time — the weeks leading up to the trade deadline — for fans … but the truth is, we really like where we’re at. Our guys are playing with a high degree of confidence and we’ll continue to move forward.”

#2796. Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to ‘a regular game'

Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to 'a regular game' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Paul George's presence back in town was one of the many swirling morning storylines at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

For good reason, former Celtics teammates Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum will draw immense attention Tuesday night. After all, Brown has said there "might be a little lamb chops with some gravy" between the two, if not a full-blown beef.

But George's return to Philadelphia is more than a tiny subplot. Following two seasons in Philadelphia, he was the centerpiece of the Sixers' summer trade package to acquire Brown.

George told reporters before the Celtics' shootaround that it feels like "a regular game."

"Had I been here eight, nine, 10 years, different story," he said. "I was here two years. Obviously, we didn't have the best of luck or had a great, I think, partnership in terms of my ability to stay healthy and on the floor. So that kind of played into a lot of it.

"But obviously, it's a tremendous fan base, a great sports town to play in. So that was appreciated. There's no ill will for me. It'll be fun to just compete. And outside of that, it's a normal game."

The 36-year-old George indeed had trouble staying available for sustained stretches as a Sixer. He dealt with a variety of injuries and served a 25-game suspension last season for violating the NBA's anti-drug policy.

George did play well on both ends of the floor for much of the 2026 playoffs. He helped the Sixers come back to win a seven-game first-round series against the Celtics in historic fashion. The nine-time All-Star also brought a veteran steadiness to the Sixers' locker room and was glad to mentor younger players like VJ Edgecombe.

"Just his routine … even when he got hot, he didn't try to force it," Jabari Walker said Tuesday morning. "His game was smooth and I think that's how he is as a person, just not forcing anything and having a calm energy."

George has certainly enjoyed his early days as a Celtic.

Through nine games, he's averaged 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals while shooting 42.5 percent from three-point range. Boston will enter Tuesday night's matchup second in the Eastern Conference standings at 8-2, the Sixers first at 9-2.

"I've been kind of blown away," George said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things and the effect it has mentally, physically, just being in an environment … and the culture that Boston presents.

"So I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball of late, and just been enjoying grinding it out with these guys out here. It's a fun style of play and I can't talk (enough) about just the thrill, the happiness and the excitement and joy that it has brought being here."

He was asked what sort of reception he expects from Sixers fans.

"Not sure," George said. "In some ways, I helped land them a future piece for them. So with that, not sure how the reception would be."

Pacers superstar Tyrese Haliburton's return gets unfortunate injury update from Rick Carlisle appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Indiana Pacers superstar guard Tyrese Haliburton's return remains on hold as he recovers from a left hamstring strain, with head coach Rick Carlisle providing another update Wednesday.

The point guard played four games before the injury interrupted his return season. Haliburton remains out for Wednesday's home matchup against the Washington Wizards at Gainbridge Fieldhouse as Indiana looks to improve its 3-8 record.

Haliburton's absence has left the Pacers without their primary playmaker after he missed the entire 2025-26 campaign with a torn right Achilles tendon. His latest recovery has extended beyond the initial two-week period Carlisle discussed in late October.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

ClutchPoints shared Carlisle's latest assessment Wednesday on X, formerly Twitter, outlining what the coach's update means for Haliburton's availability as Indiana continues its schedule without its starting point guard.

Haliburton averaged 21.8 points, 9.5 assists, and 4.5 rebounds in his first four appearances of the 2026-27 season, playing roughly 33 minutes per game.

Those games marked his return after missing the entire 2025-26 campaign. He suffered a torn right Achilles tendon during Game 7 of the 2025 NBA Finals against the Oklahoma City Thunder.

The current injury involves his left hamstring. Carlisle described the strain as mild on October 28 and listed Haliburton as week-to-week, saying a return within two weeks was unrealistic.

That period has passed without clearance for game action. Wednesday's comments offered an indication of improvement but did not establish a firm comeback date.

#2792. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2791. Guardians Urged to Sign Ex-Tigers Starting Pitcher

BOSTON, MASSACHUSETTS - APRIL 17: Casey Mize #12 of the Detroit Tigers pitches against the Boston Red Sox during the first inning at Fenway Park on April 17, 2026 in Boston, Massachusetts. (Photo by Brian Fluharty/Getty Images) Getty

CLEVELAND, OHIO - OCTOBER 03: Parker Messick #77 of the Cleveland Guardians reacts after pitching against the Chicago White Sox during the first inning in game one of the American League Division Series at Progressive Field on October 03, 2026 in Cleveland, Ohio. (Photo by Nick Cammett/Getty Images) Getty

The Cleveland Guardians made it to Game 6 of the ALCS before falling to the Tampa Bay Rays.

That sets up a key offseason, with a chance to make the right moves and get to the World Series, and that could involve changing their starting pitching rotation.

The Guardians are known for their pitching, and they'll still have a good core group back.

Foster Griffin, though, seems likely to leave in free agency after his midseason arrival from the Nationals, and so there might be at least one opening.

One MLB insider has a plan for the Guardians to fill that role.

GettyBOSTON, MASSACHUSETTS – APRIL 17: Casey Mize #12 of the Detroit Tigers pitches against the Boston Red Sox during the first inning at Fenway Park on April 17, 2026 in Boston, Massachusetts. (Photo by Brian Fluharty/Getty Images)

ESPN's Jeff Passan wrote a new article on Wednesday in which he put on the table the idea of the Guardians signing Casey Mize.

Mize was formerly a No. 1 overall pick by the Detroit Tigers out of Auburn. He remained with the Tigers until the 2026 trade deadline, when they shipped him to the San Diego Padres.

Now, Mize is a free agent and could potentially fit the Guardians' roster.

"While the Guardians' aggressiveness at the trade deadline helped them reach the ALCS, nobody expects them to replicate that in free agency," Passan writes. "With the emergence of Chase DeLauter and trade for Jo Adell, their outfield is set for the first time in years. Their infield is solid, too, with the exception of first base, and between that and designated hitter, Cleveland should be in on midtier power bats. What the Guardians really could use is one more solid starting pitcher, preferably on a shorter-term deal, with real upside. And the ideal candidate is right there for them."

Mize struggled with the Padres, but he's got a solid track record, and Cleveland sure knows how to get the most out of its pitchers.

GettyCLEVELAND, OHIO – OCTOBER 03: Parker Messick #77 of the Cleveland Guardians reacts after pitching against the Chicago White Sox during the first inning in game one of the American League Division Series at Progressive Field on October 03, 2026 in Cleveland, Ohio. (Photo by Nick Cammett/Getty Images)

If the Guardians added Mize, they'd have a full pitching rotation.

They've already got Parker Messick and Gavin Williams established at the front of the group.

Tanner Bibee and Joey Cantillo will both be back for the 2027 season, too.

With Mize, Cleveland would have a logical five-man staff to begin the new campaign and chase a World Series.

#2787. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.
