#2785. Max Verstappen questions Ferrari’s decision to let Lewis Hamilton and Charles Leclerc race freely

Following his impressive win at Autodromo Hermanos Rodriguez, Max Verstappen said he would never put two number one drivers together if he were a team principal of Ferrari. He believes the battle between Lewis Hamilton and Charles Leclerc gifted him the win in Mexico.

The Ferrari pair fought hard on the opening lap at the Mexico City Grand Prix, and Verstappen took advantage of the clash, winning the race, with Hamilton second and Leclerc fourth after a time penalty.

Asked about it by Viaplay during the Sao Paulo Grand Prix weekend, the Dutchman said the situation could have been avoided.

"I've said it before, and a lot of people often don't agree, but if I was a team principal, I'd never get two number one drivers because then you get things like this," he said.

"One won't yield for the other because they both think they can win, and that's how I am too. And if I was a team principal, I'd change this."

He added that Ferrari would have been better served with a clear pecking order.

"If you had a clear number one and number two, they would've gone through the first corner as P1 and P2 and finished like that. Or P1 and P3, that's fine too, because as a team you're still scoring great points."

Ferrari has consistently confirmed that both drivers are free to race each other. Following what became a dramatic weekend for the Scuderia, team chief Fred Vasseur held a meeting with his two drivers. He confirmed to the media that during this, they had "squashed it".

Charles Leclerc, Ferrari, Lewis Hamilton, Ferrari

Ferrari came away from Interlagos with just six points, as Leclerc and Hamilton finished eighth and ninth. The team is still second in the standings, 95 points ahead of Red Bull, with three races left.

Speaking at the circuit, Vasseur said: "We did [the meeting] yesterday evening because I had to fly back to Europe between the two races, but it went well.

"It was good to discuss, good to clarify my position and their position. It was good to do and now we turn the page and we are focused on the next one.

"The guidelines is still the same. They accumulated perhaps a bit of frustration because they are starting each weekend side-by-side and you have a kind of accumulation of fact. But at the end of the day, the guidelines is always the same.

"They need to respect each other, they are two competitors with the fighting spirit for sure, but they need to have respect for the other one, but mainly and firstly, they need to respect the brand, the team, the people working at home, the people working on track. I think we are putting a lot of effort on this, and the message was clear and will stay clear."

#2782. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2777. Celtics will be without star player vs Toronto Raptors

TORONTO — The Celtics and Raptors will each miss key players as they meet on Wednesday night.

For the Celtics, Paul George is out with an illness; the star is just a day removed from a 36-point performance against his former squad, the Philadelphia 76ers. Everyone else on the Celtics will play on the second night of a road back-to-back; the team flew from Philadelphia to Toronto late Tuesday night, and Mitchell Robinson and Jayson Tatum, both of whom have gotten one rest game this season, are among them.

George has been the Celtics' second-leading scorer so far this season; he's averaging 22 points, 5.2 rebounds, 3.8 assists, and 2.1 steals per game. He's also been the team's most efficient scorer, shooting 48.8% from the field and 44% from three. The Celtics are 0-1 without him so far this season; they lost by 16 to the Houston Rockets last Monday.

The Raptors could be significantly shorthanded; Scottie Barnes (left groin strain) and Kawhi Leonard (left knee injury management) are both out, while Trayce Jackson-Davis (illness), Colin Murray-Boyles (illness), and Immanuel Quickley (right hamstring injury management) are all questionable.

After blowing out the 76ers on Tuesday night, the Celtics lead the Eastern Conference at 9-2. The Raptors have the 6th-best record in the East at 5-5. The Celtics have won four straight; the Raptors are coming off a loss to the Memphis Grizzlies.

The Celtics are in the middle of their third back-to-back of the season, and Jayson Tatum has played in every single back-to-back game so far.

But last week in Charlotte, he said he's receptive to conversations around his rest management.

"It's my 10th year, so I understand the balance of wanting to play every game, and rest, and back-to-backs, three-in-fours, five-in-sevens," Tatum said. "It's always a conversation. We talk about it. We have a plan. I don't really like sitting out on away games, but we just kind of figure it out as we go."

Tatum said he does not like missing road games, and that has factored into his decision to play on the second nights of multiple back-to-backs this season.

"[That's] part of it, and just kind of the mental aspect of it," he said. "I used to always play back-to-backs, and obviously health is most important, and how I'm feeling physically. But it was an opportunity for me to test myself out, to see how I felt playing the back-to-backs, traveling, getting in at a certain time, and see how I felt after that. I kind of just really wanted to go through that for myself, and I felt really good."

#2776. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2772. Jaylen Brown explains apparent snub of former teammate Jayson Tatum: 'I wasn't paying attention'

Two days before playing his former team for the first time since being traded this summer, Philadelphia 76ers star Jaylen Brown told reporters he had "nothing to talk about" with ex-teammate Jayson Tatum.

Apparently he meant it — and it might have had something to do with lamb chops. Figuratively speaking.

Before Tuesday's game at Philadelphia's Xfinity Mobile Arena, Tatum approached Brown and gave him a tap on the arm and a few on the chest. Brown wouldn't even look in the direction of the player with whom he won an NBA championship, made two NBA Finals appearances and played in five Eastern Conference championship series during nine seasons as Celtics teammates.

After the game, a 123-101 Celtics win, Brown implied to reporters that he didn't notice Tatum's attempt to interact with him.

"I'm just focused on coming out and doing what's best for my team," Brown said when asked about the apparent snub. "Everything else was just — to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson, he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics, they executed, they shot the hell out of the ball, and they were the better team tonight."

Read more: Jaylen Brown video sparks furor over how Beverly Hills police treat Black people

Tatum also dismissed the pregame non-encounter as no big deal.

"No headlines about anything like that," Tatum told reporters. "I was just getting ready for the game, ready for jump ball, go out there and compete, have some fun and try to get a win."

Still, things have been a bit frosty between the two players in the aftermath of a July 6 trade that sent Brown to Philadelphia — after spending his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston — in exchange for Paul George, two first-round draft picks and two second-round picks.

Celtics teammates Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown share a laugh during a 2025 game against the New York Knicks in Boston. (Maddie Meyer / Getty Images)

In October, Bleacher Report posted a clip on Instagram of Tatum saying he was "caught off guard" by the trade. Brown responded in the comment section of that post with a blue hat emoji, which is often used to represent the word "cap" when being used as slang for "lie."

Asked about the situation during an Oct. 23 news conference, Tatum insisted "there's no beef" between the two players.

Read more: LeBron James says 'family is safe' after arrest of alleged stalker outside Brentwood home

"I think he's right. There's no beef," Brown told reporters. "Jason comes from a wonderful family. We've accomplished some great things. He's a hell of a basketball player. He's got a great PR team. There's no beef but, you know, it might be a little lamb chops and some gravy, you know what I mean? He should stay ready at all times."

Asked why he would say that, Brown responded: "Just, you know, that's what it is. No beef. It should never ever be that. It's just basketball. We're both blessed, but it might be a little lamb chop. We'll be all right."

Brown was then asked if he had been suggesting that Tatum was being untruthful about the trade situation.

Read more: What we've learned about the first-place Lakers

"Hey, look, like I said, it's no beef," Brown said. "The PR and all that type stuff is it is what it is, but I think we're just both focused on what's in front of us."

George was the leading scorer in Tuesday's game, with 36 points, while Tatum added 21 points, six rebounds and two blocked shots. Brown netted 13 points, his lowest output in 11 games with the 76ers.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said after the game. "I was looking forward to playing well, but you know, s— happens. You just get ready for the next one, I guess."

#2768. Caio Borralho lists trio of title-shot-worthy contenders, including himself

NEW YORK – Caio Borralho expected to face a higher-ranked opponent than Yousri Belgaroui in his next outing.

While the matchup initially didn't meet the expectations of Borralho (18-2 MMA, 8-1 UFC), he'll get the opportunity to stay active against a new challenge in Belgaroui (11-3 MMA, 3-0 UFC) at Madison Square Garden in the feature bout at middleweight (Paramount+).

For Borralho, No. 7 in the latest USA TODAY Sports/MMA Junkie middleweight rankings, agreeing to take a fight against an unranked opponent initially gave him pause.

"To be honest, in the beginning, I was like, 'It doesn't make sense,'" Borralho told reporters at media day. "Because I was calling Dricus (du Plessis), I was supposed to fight (Joe) Pyfer, and all that. At the end of the day, when I stopped to think about this fight, I was kind of, 'That's the fight.' There's no one else to fight. I've fought most of the guys in the top 15, I won most of them. There was no fight until January, February, so I wanted to stay active."

Borralho is coming off a unanimous decision win over Reinier de Ridder at UFC 326 in March, a successful bounce-back after his first loss in the promotion to Nassourdine Imaov last September. Despite not having a number next to his name, Belgaroui has turned heads in just three UFC appearances. He's stopped each opponent by strikes, which is something Borralho has been studying closely – although there's more to his game.

"I think he's a very good striker for sure," Borallho said. "Everybody knows that. He has all these titles in kickboxing and all that, so he's a very good striker. He knows how to manage distance. He's very tall, and this is something new (for me), but the thing that I notice on him, that he does very good against wrestlers. He knows how to get up really good, how to fight the hands all the time, how to use the cage in his favor. So, I think he has all these good attributes on his game that I need to figure out on Saturday."

With a win over Belgaroui, Borralho believes he would maintain his position among a shortlist of contenders ready for a shot at the middleweight title. With Sean Strickland's next title defense expected against Nassourdine Imaov, Borralho believes it's important to stay active and be ready for any opportunity to earn the next title shot.

"We have three big names right now to go to the belt," Borralho said. "I don't think Khamzat (Chimaev) deserves right now to go to the belt. I think we have Pyfer, Brendan Allen, and myself. I think the three of us are doing a very good run, we are facing the best guys, and any three of us in the mix. ... After my win on Saturday, we might fight each other and see whoever goes first."

Of course, Jusuf Nurkić still hasn't made his season debut due to a toe injury.

That has left Utah shorthanded early on this year, which has contributed to their 1-10 start. However, especially recently, that has allowed others to thrive. Darryn Peterson has stepped up in a big way lately, with the first three 20-point games of his career coming over Utah's last three games. The hot starts of other rookies have overshadowed Peterson, but if he continues to produce to this level, he'll quickly remind everyone why he was the No. 2 overall pick and will be one of the best rookies in fantasy basketball.

Ace Bailey has also gotten the chance to shine, though not in the way that many expected when he was drafted. He was supposed to just be a shot-creating scorer, but he has done more as a playmaker and as a defender, which has worked out well for fantasy managers. That doesn't mean he can't score, which he proved on Tuesday by scoring a season-high 23 points. Isaiah Collier didn't start in place of George, with Josh Okogie getting the nod instead and Peterson playing point guard. However, Collier was productive off the bench with a 17/5/5 line that included four triples. Even if he's coming off the bench, Collier can continue to be streamed, assuming George remains out.

Thankfully, there aren't too many other injury updates around the league. Hopefully that's a trend that continues.

Jabari Falls, Colon — Falls helped Colon beat Climax-Scotts on the road Friday in a regional win. He carried the ball 26 times for 181 yards and two scores.

Logan Stuart, Mendon — Stuart only ran for 46 yards, but he scored twice on the ground for Mendon. He also caught an 80-yard TD pass from Owen Klinger and intercepted a pair of passes.

#2759. With all eyes on the Jays, Paul George lands first blow in renewed Sixers-Celtics rivalry

They say hindsight is 20/20, but was there anything more obvious — or perhaps anything more Sixers — than the night that was supposed to centered around Jaylen Brown vs. Jayson Tatum being dominated by the salary the Sixers sent out to Boston to acquire Brown?

Paul George's 36 points led a Celtics three-point onslaught that allowed them to defeat the Sixers by 22 points in Brown's first game against his old team since the blockbuster trade in the summer. While emotions were running high for a lot of the night's participants, George didn't feel any extra need to make a statement against the team he spent the last two seasons with.

"My job was just to come in and support JT and be the best version that I could be for him," George said after the game. "That was my only focus."

There really doesn't get much better support than going 6-of-11 from three-point range. The Sixers were frustrated with their inability to stop George getting open from behind the arc, especially his former teammates who know him well.

"He played within the game too, that's what Paul does. He plays within the game, he takes what the defense gives him, and we gave him too many open looks tonight," Tyrese Maxey said. "For him to even be able to get up 11 threes, that's the issue. We know what PG does, we shouldn't even been allowing him to get up 11 threes, and that's on us."

Maxey, along with several other Sixers, were happy to reconnect with a teammate they got along with well during his time. The sting of what PG had just done to them on the floor was still on their minds when asked what it was like to see him again though.

"It's good to see Paul," Maxey said. "I didn't want to see Paul get six threes, 30 points. I love him, love his family, I didn't care less, I wanted him to score zero points."

As someone who went through similar injury struggles, Joel Embiid always seemed to have a good relationship with George. He detailed that while in classic Embiid fashion, trolling Tatum a little bit in the process.

"Off the court, we have a special relationship. Families are close, especially both of our kids," Embiid said. "I'm extremely happy for him. You know, he's healthy. He's playing well. He's been … the start of the season, you see what he did tonight. You can kind of say he's been the best player on some level."

George seemed happy to reunite with old teammates as well, referring to them as brothers throughout the night, but his return was never going to elicit the same emotion as lamb chops and gravy. That might have been different if he had been a Sixer longer, but as he told reporters at shoot around before the game, he was only in Philadelphia for two years.

The Sixer crowd didn't give him the same level of venom that's typically gone to star players since departing, James Harden in particular has really heard it from Sixers' crowds each time he's come back. Most of the crowd's animosity was aimed at Tatum, but George still had to deal with some rotten fan behavior. Towards the end of the game, the Sixers called a timeout after another Celtics three and George was seen giving the middle finger towards the crowd.

It turns out that despite being a Celtic, George was pretty justified in that response.

"There was a fan being an asshole. Ball rolled to him, he looked me dead in my eyes, dropped the ball on the ground as if, I'm a dog to go fetch or go get it, instead of passing the ball to me like a normal fan would do," George said, "so then I rolled him a middle finger in return."

Despite all the new exciting parts of this team, a max player coming back to light up the Sixers in his return to South Philadelphia is a story all too familiar.

At least George isn't the type of guy to scream "Jaylen Brown over me??!!" down the tunnel after doing so.

#2754. Nolan Teasley: This roster has "earned the right" to maximize its potential with Kirk Cousins

On Tuesday, the Vikings worked the phones and ultimately found a new starting quarterback in Kirk Cousins. On Wednesday, the work began to get Cousins up to speed.

Although G.M. Nolan Teasley deferred to coach Kevin O’Connell as to the new depth chart with Cousins in the room, Teasley also stated the obvious in a press conference with reporters.

“We . . . didn’t acquire Kirk to not play,” Teasley said.

Teasley pointed to various factors that drew the Vikings to a reunion with Cousins, including his familiarity with the offense, the organization, and the coaching staff. Teasley also noted Cousins’s “live arm,” touch, placement, and accuracy.

Teasley also tried to push back against the perception (if not reality) that the Monday night disaster against the Bills prompted a Tuesday fire drill that ended with the trade for Cousins.

“I would tell you that it didn’t,” Teasley said regarding the impact of the Week 9 game on the outcome. “We’ve been looking for ways to improve this football team, again, for weeks. And we just followed the process. It was a wide net that we were casting. And ultimately this is what we landed on, and this was the right move for this point in time.”

Right, but it’s hard not to make the connection between Monday night’s showing and the Tuesday trade deadline. Indeed, if the trade deadline hadn’t previously been shifted from the Tuesday after Week 8 to the Tuesday after Week 9, the window may have closed before the Vikings realized how badly they needed to jump through it.

At the end of the day, it’s clear that the Vikings felt compelled to get the most out of what the team currently has.

“This roster has earned the right to explore the potential to maximize where we can take this thing,” Teasley said.

Could Cousins stay with the Vikings beyond the current season?

“There’s always potential for that, but this is really about 2026.” Teasley said. “And that’s where we’re focused. . . . When that time comes we’ll address it.”

For now, they addressed getting more out of the side of the ball that’s struggling mightily.

“We still believe in the potential of the offense,” Teasley said. “There’s a lot of really talented players over there. So this is about doing what’s best for the Vikings in totality.”

Teasley opted not to engage in a hypothetical discussion as to how Cousins impacts the team’s ceiling.

“This is a really good football team,” Teasley said. “We’re 6-2 through eight games, and we just acquired a really good football player.”

While Teasley won’t address hypotheticals, others will. Without Cousins, the Vikings were destined to implode. They wouldn’t have won enough games to make it to the playoffs.

Now? They’re in position to take the NFC North — and possibly to win a playoff game or two. If, as it appears, the trade rejuvenates the defense and elevates the offense, the Vikings will become one of the most balanced teams in the league.

Which will give them an opportunity to navigate what will be the meat grinder of the looming NFC playoff tree.

#2752. NFL Injury Report, Week 10: Latest news, updates for Puka Nacua, George Pickens, more players for fantasy football

It's the halfway point of the 2026 NFL season and by now you likely have weathered plenty of injuries on your fantasy football rosters. We have a handful of big names we're tracking going into Week 10 while four teams are on bye — the Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers and Eagles. Below we'll update you on the latest injury news, plus provide some useful fantasy football analysis.

Sam Darnold, SEA (quad/ribs) — Out for Week 10

Drew Lock, SEA (oblique) — Should be good to start

Deshaun Watson, CLE (concussion) — Set to start

Gardner Minshew, ARI (elbow) — On IR with torn UCL

Mahomes: At the time of Mahomes' injury in Week 9, it looked as though we were watching a QB who may be done for the season. But after exiting due to an elbow injury, Mahomes was able to return in the Chiefs' 35-12 win over the Jets on Sunday. Mahomes completed 23-of-31 passes for 242 yards and two scores, posting fewer than 20 fantasy points for the fifth time in the past six games.

Kenneth Walker III continues to be the focal point of the offense as the passing game lacks depth and a vertical threat. The Chiefs face the Falcons in Week 10 and Mahomes was a full practice participant Wednesday, a good sign for him being able to play. If he's able to go, it'll be a chance for him to work with new receiver Josh Downs, who came over in a trade with the Colts on Tuesday.

The Raiders have been a great fantasy matchup for QBs this season, allowing the fifth-most points to the position. Brady Cook will be the backup.

#2751. Big Mitch quietly showed exactly why the Celtics signed him

Tuesdays' big-stage matchup between the Boston Celtics and Philadelphia 76ers saw no shortage of storylines. Buried beneath the Jaylen Brown vs. Jayson Tatum, Celtics vs. Sixers rivalry, and the battle of the uncs, Mitchell Robinson quietly entered his first real measuring stick game.

What did he have for Boston as they matched up against Joel Embiid (alongside four other massive names)?

Adding Robinson over the summer came with plenty of luxrious hypotheticals. That's all great. More than anything, Boston clearly needed some extra frontcourt help after their previous rotation of Neemias Queta, Nikola Vucevic, and Luka Garza had no answer for post-appendectomy Embiid in the playoffs last spring.

Queta spent much of the series in foul trouble, while neither Vucevic nor Garza was strong enough to keep the former NBA MVP away from the basket. Buckets came easy for The Process and so did the wins.

Embiid dominated the Celtics in Games 5-7 to help lead the Sixers to a 3-1 comeback in what was their first playoff series win over Boston in 44 years.

Big Mitch hasn't quite met the expectations surrounding his arrival just yet. He hasn't been the same nuisance on the offensive glass as he was seemingly everytime he'd play Boston as a member of the New York Knicks. He's looked out of shape. His quickness and defensive playmaking have remained hidden talents.

Plus, the "Sheets FC" analytics nerd portion of the fanbase would tell you that those numbers don't favor Celtics Mitch just yet.

You wouldn't have been able to guess all that if you just watched him for the first time on Tuesday. Robinson clearly understood the assignment.

It's not always easy to notice defense when you watch a game live. Sometimes you're just watching the ball. Others, the fundamental, non-highlight-worthy defense doesn't jump off the screen at you. The experience is a little different when Embiid catches the ball in the post and you get war flashbacks to the spring.

You just kind of expect him to take a few power dribbles and work his was to the rim.

Instead, Robinson consistently held his ground and forced the longtime 76er to work further from the basket. He also remained under control while getting physical — an important skill when guarding one of the league's most profound grifters. Like, Embiid averages more free-throws per game this season than field-goal attempts.

So, the fact that Mitch was able to defend him without fouling (except for when he decided to sloppily closeout on a three-point attempt for some reason, despite Embiid shooting 10% from deep entering the game) was huge.

The Louisiana native guarded the seven-foot-two center on 24.3 partial possessions, held him to five points on 1-3 from the field, and forced a turnover. Embiid couldn't move him. It was like watching him try to back down one of Mitch's monster trucks.

For perspective, Queta guarded Embiid on 18.1 partial possessions in Boston's Game 7 loss to Philly back in May. Embiid scored 14 points on Queta while shooting an efficient 6-11 from the floor.

The Portuguese big man was much better Tuesday, in fairness. Queta limited Embiid to just three points on 17 partial possessions.

Both his play, and Robinson's as his backup, were a clear step in the right direction. There's a lot of season left. Robinson has to continue to uphold a defensive standard. But, if he looks the way he did against Embiid and the 76ers, then Boston will have aptly plugged the hole that sank their ship last spring.

#2746. By the numbers: What Cooper Flagg is doing isn’t normal

After an impressive Rookie of the Year campaign in the 2025-2026 season, Cooper Flagg entered this year looking to polish his offensive game and bring the Dallas Mavericks back to relevance. So far, he's exceeded expectations in both.

Through 11 games, the Mavericks are 7-4 and have faced one of the league's tougher schedules. Flagg has played in 10 of those games and is averaging 25.9 points, 6.9 rebounds, 6.9 assists, and 2.2 steals per game. He's 19 years old. He joins a short list of only Luka Doncic and LeBron James as one of the youngest players in NBA history to average at least 25 points, 5 rebounds, and 5 assists per game. At 20 years old, Doncic averaged 28.8 points, 9.4 rebounds, and 8.8 assists per game, while James, at 20, averaged 27.2 points, 7.4 rebounds, and 7.2 assists.

What's been most impressive about his leap is Flagg's shooting. He's knocking down 50.0% of his shots, on 61.2% true shooting. He's also been excellent at the free-throw line, shooting 90.3% there. Put those numbers together, and you get a player whose scoring efficiency is looking much more advanced than you might expect from a 19-year-old. Let's take a deeper dive into the numbers.

Flagg has attempted 42 shots at the rim (in the restricted area), making 28 of them for 66.7%. That's roughly 22% of his total attempts. It's not an enormous portion of his shot diet, but he can get there whenever he wants. Flagg often uses his size and athleticism to his advantage. At 6-foot-9, he can finish with ease over smaller defenders but also has the finesse to finish around larger ones. That combination makes his drives difficult to contain. Unlike some young perimeter players who settle for difficult jumpers when their initial attack gets cut off, Flagg has multiple ways he can beat you off the dribble.

Flagg has taken 47 shots in the portion of the paint outside of the restricted area, the most of any zone on the floor. He's made 27 of them, giving him a 57.4% shooting percentage. That's roughly 26% of all his field-goal attempts. The non-restricted-area paint has become something of a sweet spot for Flagg because it allows him to use his size and touch without having to completely beat the defense to the rim. It's also a shot that can be available when a defense successfully cuts off his initial drive. His comfortability to get to this part of the floor and shoot over defenders has proven to be his bread and butter.

Nearly half of Flagg's shot attempts come inside the paint, and he's incredibly efficient, shooting 61.7% on those attempts, 55-89. A teenager being this comfortable living in the paint is pretty rare, especially in today's NBA, where outside shooting rules. Once Flagg expands his game beyond the three-point arc, he'll be unstoppable.

Flagg has Kevin Durant-like capabilities in his mid-range game. He has attempted 43 mid-range shots, second only to his 47 attempts in the non-restricted-area paint. He's made 21, good for 48.8%. That's an important number because the mid-range is often treated as something players should eliminate from their offensive diet. For Flagg, that's not necessarily the case. Use Durant as the example, who is currently fifth on the all-time NBA scoring leaders list, as he approaches 33,000 points.

At nearly 49%, Flagg is making enough of these shots to justify taking them — particularly because many of them are self-created. If a defender backs up to protect the drive, Flagg can shoot. If the defender presses up, he can attack. If the help defender steps into the lane, he can stop short. That puts a lot of pressure on the defense and leaves them with no real solution in guarding him.

There's a tendency in modern basketball analysis to look at a player's shot chart and immediately identify mid-range attempts as something that needs to be reduced. That isn't necessarily true for Flagg. An efficient mid-range shooter who can also get to the rim, shoot threes and create for teammates is a completely different proposition than a player who lives in the mid-range because he can't do anything else. Flagg is not that player. The mid-range is one part of his offensive toolkit, and right now, it's a pretty good one.

Now for the part nobody needs to sugarcoat – 29.2% from three isn't good, making 14 of 48. The shot-chart data shows that almost all of Flagg's three-point attempts are coming above the break. He has attempted 42 above-the-break threes and made only 10, good for 23.8%. That's the biggest blemish on his shooting profile. The good news is that he's actually been excellent from the corners. Flagg has attempted just six corner threes, but he's made four of them for 66.7%. Obviously, that's an extremely small sample, but worth noting. But it does reinforce an important point: the problem isn't that Flagg can't physically make a three. It's consistency, particularly on higher-volume, above-the-break attempts.

One thing not to lose in the discussion about his three-point percentage is the fact that Flagg is willing to take them. He's attempted 48 total threes. That's enough volume to matter. He's not hiding from the shot. He's not passing up every open look. He's taking the shots defenses are giving him. If he can get this percentage to 34-36%, Flagg will enter 30 points per game territory.

It's too early to call Flagg a great shooter, but at 19 he is a great scorer. Shooting wasn't his calling card coming into the NBA, but it's evolving. Being an efficient scorer in today's NBA without a reliable three-point shot (yet), bodes well for Flagg. As he continues to work on his outside shooting and that three-point percentage creeps northward, he will become one of the most unstoppable forces in the NBA. The three-point shot isn't the foundation of Cooper Flagg's offensive game. It's the potential multiplier.

Flagg arrived on the heels of Luka Doncic's departure to Los Angeles. And despite the weight of seismic expectations and a tumultuous situation when he arrived, Flagg has largely exceeded expectations. These numbers aren't normal for a teenager. They point to something much larger – the next generational superstar.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2745. Reviewing the Bears’ PFN grades from Week 9 vs. Buccaneers

The Bears extended their winning streak to four games on Sunday night, squeezing away with a 27-24 win over the Buccaneers at home. They improved their record to 7-2 in the process, and though their offensive performance wasn't pretty, their strong defense led them to victory. As they prepare to head into their bye week in Week 10, they have a well-timed opportunity to rest up and regroup.

Each week, I'll review how Chicago's eligible performers graded by Pro Football Network's Impact Scoring model. Now that the rest of the NFL has caught up, and the numbers have all been crunched, here's how Week 9 looked.

Analytically, Caleb Williams had his worst game of the season since his Week 2 outing in the rain against the Vikings. His -0.21 EPA per dropback marked the worst average he's had in a single game all year. His four interceptions certainly played a major role in his reaching a negative average for the first time in 2026. That said, he still finished with an above-average completion percentage over expected, coming in No. 13 with a +1.0 percentage.

It's not much of a surprise that the rest of the Bears' offense didn't grade incredibly well on Sunday. The offensive interior struggled in particular, with Garrett Bradbury finishing well below average at center and Joe Thuney taking his lumps at left guard. Darnell Wright continued his stellar 2026, though, as his 89.0 PFN OL Impact grade for the year leads all offensive tackles in the NFL.

Clark Phillips III: 79.5 (21st of 101 CBs)

Tyrique Stevenson: 72.7 (63rd of 101 CBs)

Chicago's defense played a huge role in its win over the Buccaneers on Sunday, and it was highlighted by interceptions from both Jaylon Johnson and Cam Lewis. Johnson returned his interception back for a touchdown, and he allowed a flawless 0.0 passer rating when targeted in coverage in Week 9. Considering the rocky season he's had in 2026 thus far, putting together such a strong outing had to mean a lot to him. It certainly meant a lot to the Bears.

Elsewhere, the defensive standouts included Jeffery Simmons — who had another sack in his second game with the Bears — Austin Booker, and Devin Bush. Bush cleaned up with nine tackles, thriving in space as Simmons plugged up running lanes in front of him. The emergence of Clark Phillips III is a particularly interesting statistic, as he allowed a targeted passer rating of just 39.6 in coverage on Sunday.

Though Cairo Santos deserves credit for coming through in the clutch with his 38-yard field goal to win the Bears the game, the relative lack of difficulty of his kicks doesn't help his analytical profile much. His other converted field goal came from just 25 yards out, so other NFL kickers grade higher thanks to superior field goals made over expected. On the punting side of things, Tory Taylor averaged just 40.3 net yards per punt, which ranked No. 23 among 29 punters in Week 9.

#2744. J.J. McCarthy, Cam Skattebo have no injury designations for Thursday night

The Giants opened the week with confidence that quarterback J.J. McCarthy and running back Cam Skattebo would be ready to play against the Commanders on Thursday night and nothing changed on that front over the last couple of days.

Neither McCarthy nor Skattebo received an injury designation on Wednesday, which leaves both of them on track to play as the Giants try to improve to 6-2-1 on the season.

McCarthy briefly left last Sunday’s win over the Eagles with a hip injury, but returned for the fourth quarter and moved up to a full practice listing on Tuesday. Skattebo missed the Eagles game with the hamstring injury he suffered in Week 7 and also moved up to full participation the last two days.

Linebacker Malik Harrison is the only Giants player with a designation. He’s questionable to play due to a groin injury.
